L1 - Earth orientation parameters

referenceSystem.readEop

Read an IERS Earth orientation parameter series into the eop struct.

SYNTAX

eop = readEop(file)
  eop = readEop(file, 'Format', "EOP 20 C04")
  [eop, info] = readEop(file, ...)

  Parses one of the supported IERS Earth orientation series and returns the
  eop struct of handover section B.3, with every quantity converted to SI at
  read time and a fully populated meta substruct. Nothing downstream of this
  function ever sees an arcsecond or a milliarcsecond.

INPUT ARGUMENTS

Name    Size   Type            Units  Description
  ----    ----   ----            -----  -----------
  file    1x1    string | char   -      Path to the series file.

OPTIONAL NAME-VALUE ARGUMENTS

Name       Size  Type    Default   Description
  ----       ----  ----    -------   -----------
  Format     1x1   string  "auto"    Series format. "auto" identifies it from
                                     the header; the explicit values are
                                     "EOP 14 C04", "EOP 20 C04",
                                     "EOP 20 C04 dPsiDEpsilon" and
                                     "finals2000A". An unidentifiable file is
                                     an error, never a default.
  CipModel   1x1   string  "auto"    Precession-nutation model the published
                                     pole offsets refer to. "auto" takes the
                                     registry value; "IAU2006/2000A" or
                                     "IAU2000A" override it. See METHOD.
  Bulletin   1x1   string  "A"       finals2000A only: which solution to
                                     read, "A" (Bulletin A, complete,
                                     includes predictions) or "B" (Bulletin
                                     B, final, past dates only). Bulletin B
                                     publishes xp, yp, UT1-UTC, dX and dY
                                     only; lod and every sigma come back NaN
                                     rather than borrowed from Bulletin A.

OUTPUT ARGUMENTS

Name              Size   Type     Units  Description
  ----              ----   ----     -----  -----------
  eop               1x1    struct   -      The series. Fields below.
  .mjd              Mx1    double   d      Modified Julian Date of each row,
                                           UTC, at the sampled hour. Decimal,
                                           not integer: the 12h-sampled C04
                                           products exist and land on .5.
  .xp, .yp          Mx1    double   rad    Polar motion.
  .dut1             Mx1    double   s      UT1 - UTC.
  .lod              Mx1    double   s      Excess length of day.
  .dX, .dY          Mx1    double   rad    CIP offsets from the model named
                                           by meta.cipModel.
  .sigma            1x1    struct   -      Formal errors, fields xp, yp,
                                           dut1, lod, dX, dY, same units.
  .meta             1x1    struct   -      Provenance. Fields below.
  .meta.series      1x1    string   -      Format name as identified.
  .meta.cipModel    1x1    string   -      "IAU2006/2000A" or "IAU2000A".
  .meta.cipModelSource
                    1x1    string   -      "format registry" or "caller".
                                           Never "file header": see METHOD.
  .meta.cipModelStatement
                    1x1    string   -      What the file itself says, verbatim,
                                           or "" where it says nothing.
  .meta.poleOffsetType
                    1x1    string   -      "observed dXdY" or
                                           "converted from dPsiDEpsilon".
  .meta.itrf        1x1    string   -      Frame realization the series is
                                           consistent with.
  .meta.file        1x1    string   -      Absolute path read.
  .meta.readTime    1x1    datetime -      When it was read.
  .meta.nRows       1x1    double   -      Number of data rows kept.
  .meta.mjdRange    1x2    double   d      First and last MJD.
  .meta.samplingHour
                    1x1    double   h      UTC hour the series is sampled at.
  .meta.bulletin    1x1    string   -      "A", "B" or "" where not applicable.
  info              1x1    struct   -      Reading diagnostics, never printed.
  .rowsRead         1x1    double   -      Rows matching the data pattern.
  .rowsKept         1x1    double   -      Rows with usable values.
  .rowsIncomplete   1x1    double   -      Rows dropped for missing values.
  .gapCount         1x1    double   -      Sampling gaps longer than one step.
  .largestGap       1x1    double   d      Longest gap, zero where none.
  .duration         1x1    double   s      Wall-clock time of the read.

METHOD

Finding M-6 is what this function replaces. v1 read a 23 000-row file with
  fgetl into a growing cell array, identified the header by sniffing for the
  substring ' DX ', supported neither the 20 C04 nor the finals series, and
  printed unconditionally on every call. Here the file is read in one
  readmatrix or one fixed-width slice, the format comes from an explicit
  registry rather than a substring, and nothing is printed: diagnostics leave
  through info, warnings through warning identifiers.

  The registry is the local function localFormatRegistry. Each entry names,
  for one published series, the column map, the unit of every column, the
  consistent ITRF realization, the kind of pole offset published, and the
  precession-nutation model. Adding a series is one entry; nothing else in
  the toolbox changes. Detection reads the header, and a file that matches no
  entry is an error, referenceSystem:readEop:unknownFormat. It is never
  guessed, because guessing a column map produces a plausible series that is
  wrong by whatever the columns happen to mean.

  THE CIP MODEL, which is finding C-2 and the reason this struct has a meta
  field at all. v1 assumed the published dX and dY were referred to IAU2000A
  and formed a per-epoch bias against IAU2006/2000A on that assumption. If
  the assumption is wrong the bias is injected rather than removed.

  Measured in session S2 with SOFA iauXys06a against iauXys00a, the two
  models differ by 0 at J2000.0, by -2 uas in X and -37 uas in Y at 2007, and
  by -125 uas in X and -137 uas in Y at 2025, growing secularly. At the
  Earth's surface 137 uas is 4.2 mm, which is exactly the level this toolbox
  exists to get right.

  No IERS file examined states which of the two it uses. The 14 C04 header
  names its columns dX and dY and says nothing more; both 20 C04 products
  carry the line "Reference Precession-Nutation Model: IAU 2000", and they
  carry it identically on the CIO-based dX/dY product and on the equinox-based
  dPsi/dEpsilon product of the same solution, which is why that line is
  recorded verbatim in meta.cipModelStatement but is not read as a model
  reference. finals2000A has no header at all.

  The registry value is therefore a stated decision, not a discovery, and
  meta.cipModelSource says so by returning "format registry" and never "file
  header". The default is IAU2006/2000A, because the C04 solutions of the
  ITRF2014 and ITRF2020 era are produced within the IERS Conventions (2010)
  framework whose recommended model is IAU 2006/2000A. Measured support for
  treating all supported series alike: differencing finals2000A against
  EOP 20 C04 over the 9256 days they share since J2000 shows no trace of the
  500 uas/century signature the two models differ by, so whichever model it
  is, these products share it.

  What has changed since v1 is not the confidence but the cost of being
  wrong. The model reference is now one field, set in one place, overridable
  with 'CipModel', carried with the data and checked by the consumer. Being
  wrong is a one-line correction instead of a rebuild.

  dPsi/dEpsilon series (risk R13c) are converted to dX/dY here, so that
  eop.dX always means dX, using the first-order relations of Capitaine and
  Wallace (2006) and IERS Technical Note 36 Chapter 5,

      dX = dPsi * sin(eps0),      dY = dEpsilon

  with eps0 = 84381.406 arcseconds. meta.poleOffsetType then reads "converted
  from dPsiDEpsilon" rather than "observed dXdY", so a consumer can tell a
  converted offset from a measured one. v1 warned and zeroed these files,
  which silently degraded the CIP correction to nothing at all.

  Rows are validated rather than trusted. The MJD column must be strictly
  increasing; a gap longer than one sampling step raises one aggregated
  warning. Every value is range-checked against what the quantity can
  physically be, which is what catches a column map applied to the wrong
  file: a shifted map produces values that are numerically fine and
  physically absurd, and the range gate is the only thing between that and a
  plausible wrong answer.

ACCURACY

Reading is exact. The values are those published, converted to SI by
  multiplication with an exact factor, so no accuracy is lost or gained here.

  The dPsi/dEpsilon conversion is first order and therefore not exact, and
  the size of what it neglects has been MEASURED rather than estimated, by
  converting the IERS 20 C04 dPsi/dEpsilon product and comparing it with the
  dX/dY product of the same solution:

      mean residual                              0.3 uas
      regression slope against the published dX  1.003
      neglected second-order term                1.2 uas

  So the conversion carries no bias and no scale error. The neglected term is
  (psiA*cos(eps0) - chiA)*dEpsilon in dX and the corresponding term in dY,
  where psiA*cos(eps0) - chiA is about 0.0224*t radians for t in Julian
  centuries from J2000.0; over the 2016-2017 span measured it reaches 1.2 uas
  and it grows linearly with t.

  For scale: had the obliquity or the trigonometric function been wrong, the
  regression slope would read 2.514 or 2.307 rather than 1.003. That is the
  quantity the precision test asserts, because it separates a conversion
  error from the interpolation noise that dominates any direct comparison of
  two products sampled twelve hours apart.

  The CIP model reference is not an accuracy statement but a correctness
  statement, and the cost of the registry default being wrong is quantified
  in METHOD: up to 137 uas, 4.2 mm at the surface, growing secularly.

  finals2000A is not internally homogeneous: its early decades were produced
  against earlier ITRF realizations than meta.itrf names. For work at the
  millimetre level use a C04 series, which is a consistent reprocessing.

ERRORS

referenceSystem:readEop:unknownFormat
  referenceSystem:readEop:noData
  referenceSystem:readEop:notMonotonic
  referenceSystem:readEop:implausibleValue
  referenceSystem:readEop:unsupportedBulletin

REFERENCES

IERS Conventions (2010), IERS Technical Note 36, Ch. 5.
  Capitaine, N. and Wallace, P. T., 2006, Astron. Astrophys. 450, 855.
  IERS EOP Product Centre, C04 series description,
    https://hpiers.obspm.fr/eoppc/eop/eopc04/

EXAMPLE

eop = referenceSystem.readEop("eopc04_20.1962-now");
  fprintf('%s, %s, %d rows\n', eop.meta.series, eop.meta.cipModel, eop.meta.nRows);

KEYWORDS

EOP, Earth orientation, C04, finals2000A, polar motion, UT1, dX, dY

See also INTERPOLATEEOP, DOWNLOADEOP, SUBDAILYEOPCORRECTION.

referenceSystem.interpolateEop

Interpolate an EOP series to a vector of epochs.

SYNTAX

eopAtEpoch = interpolateEop(eop, epoch)
  eopAtEpoch = interpolateEop(eop, epoch, 'Correction', correction)
  eopAtEpoch = interpolateEop(eop, epoch, 'Method', "spline")

  Interpolates the series returned by readEop to the UTC epochs given, once
  for the whole arc, and returns the values in SI with the metadata of the
  source series intact. UT1 is interpolated in a leap-second-safe way and
  tagged corrections are applied here rather than by the caller.

INPUT ARGUMENTS

Name    Size   Type     Units  Description
  ----    ----   ----     -----  -----------
  eop     1x1    struct   -      Series from readEop, meta included.
  epoch   1x1    struct   -      Epoch struct, N entries, scale UTC.

OPTIONAL NAME-VALUE ARGUMENTS

Name                 Size  Type      Default   Description
  ----                 ----  ----      -------   -----------
  Method               1x1   string    "linear"  "linear" or "spline".
  ExtrapolationPolicy  1x1   string    "error"   "error", "hold" or "nan".
  Sigma                1x1   logical   false     Also interpolate the formal
                                                 errors, for error propagation.
  Correction           1xK   struct    none      Tagged correction structs
                                                 from subdailyEopCorrection
                                                 or zonalTideEopCorrection.
  Regularize           1x1   logical   false     Permit a correction whose
                                                 sense is "regularization".
                                                 See METHOD before setting it.

OUTPUT ARGUMENTS

Name                  Size   Type     Units  Description
  ----                  ----   ----     -----  -----------
  eopAtEpoch            1x1    struct   -      Values at the epochs.
  .xp, .yp              Nx1    double   rad    Polar motion.
  .dut1                 Nx1    double   s      UT1 - UTC.
  .lod                  Nx1    double   s      Excess length of day.
  .dX, .dY              Nx1    double   rad    CIP offsets.
  .sigma                1x1    struct   -      Present only with Sigma=true.
  .meta                 1x1    struct   -      eop.meta, plus the fields below.
  .meta.method          1x1    string   -      Interpolation method used.
  .meta.extrapolationPolicy
                        1x1    string   -      Policy in force.
  .meta.nExtrapolated   1x1    double   -      Epochs outside the table.
  .meta.corrections     1xK    string   -      Source of each correction
                                               applied, "" where none.
  .meta.regularized     1x1    logical  -      True where a regularization
                                               correction was applied, so
                                               dut1 is UT1R - UTC.

METHOD

The whole arc is interpolated in one call. Interpolating per epoch is a
  defect, not a style choice: it turns one sorted lookup into N of them.

  LEAP-SECOND-SAFE UT1, which is handover approximation #4 and the single
  most likely defect in this layer. UT1 - UTC steps by one second at a leap
  second, so interpolating it directly smears a fraction of that second
  across the surrounding days. UT1 - TAI is smooth across the step, so the
  integer TAI - UTC is removed from the tabulated values before
  interpolation and the value at the query epoch is added back. This is what
  the IERS interp.f utility does. v1 did it too, and did it with a hardcoded
  leap table of its own, frozen at 2017 (finding M-2); the table here is
  leapSecondTable, which is the only leap-second authority in the toolbox, and
  the lookup is vectorised rather than an arrayfun per row.

  THE CORRECTION SENSE CONTRACT, decision D30. Two corrections exist and they
  are not interchangeable:

    subdailyEopCorrection    sense "additive"        applied by default
    zonalTideEopCorrection   sense "regularization"  refused by default

  The first supplies signal that a daily series does not contain: the diurnal
  and semidiurnal ocean tide and libration terms, sampled out by a series
  tabulated once a day. Adding it is what makes the series usable at
  sub-daily resolution, which is why it is accepted without ceremony.

  The second is the opposite. A C04 series already contains the zonal tide
  signal, as observed, so the zonal correction is not missing signal to add
  but existing signal to remove, and only for a user who specifically wants
  regularised UT1R. Applying it as though it were additive is a large error
  in the wrong direction, and it is invisible: the result is a
  plausible-looking rotation.

  Documentation is not sufficient protection against that, so the correction
  carries a sense tag that travels with the numbers and cannot be lost the way
  a comment can, and this function refuses a "regularization" correction
  unless 'Regularize' is true. The sign, stated because getting it backwards
  is the whole hazard: with Regularize true the correction is SUBTRACTED, so
  that dut1 becomes UT1R - UTC, and meta.regularized records that it happened.

ACCURACY

Linear interpolation of a daily-tabulated series is the IERS-recommended
  treatment and is the default here. Its error is the curvature of the
  underlying signal over one day: about 0.1 mas peak in polar motion, which is
  3 mm at the Earth's surface, and a few microseconds in UT1. Spline
  interpolation reduces the curvature error but overshoots near gaps and
  series edges, which is why it is offered and not defaulted.

  That figure is the error of the interpolation, not of the series. The
  sub-daily signal that a daily series cannot carry at all is larger, up to
  about 0.5 mas in polar motion and 50 microseconds in UT1, and is supplied by
  subdailyEopCorrection rather than by any choice of interpolation method.

  The leap-second treatment is exact, not approximate: TAI - UTC is an integer
  number of seconds from 1972 onward and removing it is a shift, so UT1 - TAI
  is interpolated with no step and the reconstruction adds back an integer.

  Applying zonalTideEopCorrection to an unregularised series, or applying it
  with the wrong sign, costs up to 168 ms of UT1 for the full 62-term model
  and about 2.1 ms for its short-period part alone. Both measured in session
  S2 over one 18.6-year nodal period. At the equator 1 ms of UT1 is 0.465 m of
  surface displacement, so these are metre-level and tens-of-metre-level
  errors, not the millimetre-level ones the handover previously stated. This
  is why the flag exists.

  Extrapolation is not performed under any policy. "hold" clamps to the edge
  value, which is a statement that nothing is known beyond the table rather
  than a prediction; "nan" marks the epochs; "error" refuses. Polynomial
  extrapolation of an EOP series is never appropriate and is not offered.

ERRORS

referenceSystem:interpolateEop:wrongScale
  referenceSystem:interpolateEop:outOfRange
  referenceSystem:interpolateEop:wrongCorrectionSense
  referenceSystem:interpolateEop:missingMeta
  referenceSystem:interpolateEop:sizeMismatch

REFERENCES

IERS Conventions (2010), IERS Technical Note 36, Ch. 5.5.
  IERS EOP Product Centre, interp.f, the reference interpolation utility.

EXAMPLE

eop = referenceSystem.readEop("eopc04_20.1962-now");
  utc = referenceSystem.daySecondToEpoch([57754*ones(86400,1) (0:86399)'], "UTC");
  subdaily = referenceSystem.subdailyEopCorrection(utc);
  e = referenceSystem.interpolateEop(eop, utc, 'Correction', subdaily);

KEYWORDS

EOP, interpolation, UT1, leap second, polar motion, regularization

See also READEOP, SUBDAILYEOPCORRECTION, ZONALTIDEEOPCORRECTION, LEAPSECONDTABLE.

referenceSystem.subdailyEopCorrection

Sub-daily ocean tide and libration variation in the EOP.

SYNTAX

correction = subdailyEopCorrection(epoch)

  Returns the diurnal and semidiurnal variation in polar motion and UT1 that
  a daily-tabulated EOP series cannot carry, as a tagged struct whose sense
  field states that the numbers are additive. It is a pure function of time
  and needs no EOP file.

INPUT ARGUMENTS

Name    Size   Type     Units  Description
  ----    ----   ----     -----  -----------
  epoch   1x1    struct   -      Epoch struct, N entries, scale UTC.

OUTPUT ARGUMENTS

Name             Size   Type     Units  Description
  ----             ----   ----     -----  -----------
  correction       1x1    struct   -      Tagged correction. Fields below.
  .dxp, .dyp       Nx1    double   rad    Polar motion variation.
  .dut1            Nx1    double   s      UT1 variation.
  .sense           1x1    string   -      "additive". See METHOD.
  .source          1x1    string   -      "subdailyEopCorrection".
  .model           1x1    string   -      The three models summed.

METHOD

The sum of three IERS Conventions (2010) models, ported to C in mex/iers/
  and evaluated through the MEX gateway:

    ORTHO_EOP   diurnal and semidiurnal ocean tides in xp, yp and UT1
    PMSDNUT2    diurnal lunisolar libration in xp and yp
    UTLIBR      subdiurnal libration in UT1

  They are summed rather than returned separately because they are always
  applied together; offering them individually would invite a caller to apply
  two of the three and get a result that is wrong by the amount of the
  missing one, with nothing to indicate it.

  v1 buried these inside its C rotation core, where they could not be
  inspected, tested or plotted from MATLAB, and defaulted them off. They are
  first-class functions here and handover approximation #5 makes them default
  on for the workflow, because omitting them is a 15 mm error at the surface
  for GRACE-class work.

  THE SENSE TAG, decision D30. This function returns sense "additive", which
  is what interpolateEop accepts by default, because these terms genuinely
  are signal a daily series does not contain. Its counterpart
  zonalTideEopCorrection returns "regularization" and is refused by default.
  The tag travels with the numbers and is checked at the consumer, so that
  the two cannot be confused by a caller who reads neither help text.

  THE TIME SCALE, decision D57 and handover finding m-8. The epoch must be
  UTC. The original Fortran routines take a Modified Julian Date and their
  headers do not state its scale to better than a minute; they are nominally
  UT1 or TT quantities. UTC is used because it is the scale the EOP series is
  tabulated against, because it is what the validated v1 pipeline fed them,
  and above all because using UT1 would make this function depend on the EOP
  series it exists to correct. The cost is quantified in ACCURACY.

  A non-UTC epoch is refused rather than converted. A silent conversion is how
  a tidal model ends up driven by the wrong time scale, and a caller holding a
  TT epoch also holds everything needed to convert it deliberately.

  No dlod field. UTLIBR computes a libration LOD term and it is deliberately
  discarded, because ORTHO_EOP publishes no matching ocean-tide LOD term:
  a returned dlod would read as "the sub-daily LOD correction" and would in
  fact be a fraction of it. A partial quantity that looks complete is worse
  than an absent one (decision D55). Where the sub-daily LOD effect is needed,
  differentiate dut1.

ACCURACY

The three models reproduce the original IERS Fortran to round-off. Measured
  in session S2 by compiling the Fortran with gfortran and differencing over
  2001 epochs spanning 1960 to 2100: the largest disagreement is 1.8e-14 in
  the routines' own units, which is 4e-16 relative. ORTHO_EOP is bit-identical.

  Amplitudes: up to about 0.5 mas in polar motion and 50 microseconds in UT1,
  that is roughly 15 mm and 23 mm respectively at the Earth's surface. This is
  the size of the error made by omitting this correction, which is why the
  workflow defaults it on.

  Feeding UTC where the models nominally expect UT1 or TT (finding m-8)
  displaces the argument by at most about 70 seconds. Against periods of half
  a day and amplitudes of tens of microarcseconds this is below 0.1 uas, that
  is under 0.003 mm at the surface, four orders of magnitude below the models'
  own accuracy. It is stated because it is an undocumented approximation in
  v1, not because it is significant.

  The models themselves are conventional: they carry the accuracy of IERS
  Conventions (2010) Chapter 8, of order 10 per cent of their own amplitude in
  the ocean-tide terms.

  PMSDNUT2 is evaluated with IBAND = 1, as the original hard-codes: the ten
  quasi-diurnal terms only. The fifteen long-period terms and the secular
  drift are excluded, following IERS Conventions (2010) section 5.5.1.1, since
  a daily EOP series already carries those frequencies as observed.

ERRORS

referenceSystem:subdailyEopCorrection:wrongScale
  referenceSystem:subdailyEopCorrection:gatewayMissing

REFERENCES

IERS Conventions (2010), IERS Technical Note 36, Ch. 5.5.1 and Ch. 8.
  Ray, R. D., Steinberg, D. J., Chao, B. F., Cartwright, D. E., 1994,
    Science 264, 830 (the ocean tide model behind ORTHO_EOP).
  Brzezinski, A., 2001, and Brzezinski and Capitaine, 2003 (libration).
  IERS Conventions software ORTHO_EOP.F, PMSDNUT2.F, UTLIBR.F.

EXAMPLE

utc = referenceSystem.daySecondToEpoch([57754*ones(24,1) (0:3600:82800)'], "UTC");
  correction = referenceSystem.subdailyEopCorrection(utc);
  e = referenceSystem.interpolateEop(eop, utc, 'Correction', correction);

KEYWORDS

EOP, ocean tide, libration, sub-daily, polar motion, UT1, ORTHO_EOP

See also INTERPOLATEEOP, ZONALTIDEEOPCORRECTION, READEOP.

referenceSystem.zonalTideEopCorrection

Long-period zonal tide effect on the rotation of the Earth.

SYNTAX

correction = zonalTideEopCorrection(epoch)

  Returns the effect of the long-period zonal Earth tides on UT1, on the
  excess length of day and on the rotational speed, as a tagged struct whose
  sense field states that the numbers REGULARISE a series rather than correct
  it. It is a pure function of time and needs no EOP file.

  Read the METHOD block before using this function. Applying it in the wrong
  sense is the largest silent error available anywhere in this toolbox.

INPUT ARGUMENTS

Name    Size   Type     Units  Description
  ----    ----   ----     -----  -----------
  epoch   1x1    struct   -      Epoch struct, N entries, scale TT.

OUTPUT ARGUMENTS

Name        Size   Type     Units    Description
  ----        ----   ----     -----    -----------
  correction  1x1    struct   -        Tagged correction. Fields below.
  .dut1       Nx1    double   s        Zonal tide effect on UT1.
  .dlod       Nx1    double   s        Effect on excess length of day, per day.
  .domega     Nx1    double   rad/s    Effect on rotational speed.
  .sense      1x1    string   -        "regularization". See METHOD.
  .source     1x1    string   -        "zonalTideEopCorrection".
  .model      1x1    string   -        The model evaluated.

METHOD

A port of IERS Conventions (2010) RG_ZONT2: 62 terms combining the Yoder et
  al. (1981) elastic body tide, the Wahr and Bergen (1986) inelastic body tide
  and the Kantha et al. (1998) ocean tide models, as Chapter 8 recommends.
  Evaluated in C through the MEX gateway. This is new capability; v1 had no
  zonal tide model at all (handover approximation #6).

  WHAT THESE NUMBERS ARE FOR, decision D30, and the reason this help text
  leads with a warning. The zonal tides make the Earth spin measurably faster
  and slower over periods from five days to 18.6 years. A measured UT1 series
  such as C04 already contains that signal, because it was observed. This
  function does not supply missing signal; it supplies the signal to REMOVE if
  you want regularised UT1R, which is a smoother quantity easier to
  interpolate and to model.

  So the correct use is subtraction, and interpolateEop does the subtraction
  for you when it is asked to:

      UT1R = UT1 - dut1

  and the function therefore returns sense "regularization", which
  interpolateEop REFUSES unless 'Regularize', true is passed. Its counterpart
  subdailyEopCorrection returns "additive" and is accepted by default. That
  asymmetry is the whole protection, and it is structural rather than
  documentary because the failure it guards has no symptom: adding this to a
  C04 series produces a rotation matrix that is orthogonal, unit-determinant,
  smooth in time and wrong.

  THE TIME SCALE. The epoch must be TT, which is what RG_ZONT2's own header
  states (strictly TDB, which it says makes no significant difference: the
  two differ by under 2 ms, against a shortest period of five days). A non-TT
  epoch is refused rather than converted, for the same reason
  fundamentalArguments refuses one.

  THE THIRD OUTPUT. domega is the effect on rotational speed and is returned
  because RG_ZONT2 computes it in the same pass and because it is the natural
  input to an angular-momentum budget. It is not used by anything else in this
  toolbox and interpolateEop ignores it.

ACCURACY

The port reproduces the original IERS Fortran to 1.7e-16 relative, measured
  in session S2 over 2001 epochs spanning 1960 to 2100 against RG_ZONT2.F
  compiled with gfortran, and reproduces the routine's published test case
  exactly in dut1 and to 4e-20 in dlod.

  HOW BIG THE MISTAKE IS, measured over one 18.6-year nodal period, because
  decision D30 rests on this number and it must not be a remembered one:

    full 62-term model, peak |dut1|          167.7 ms   = 78 m at the equator
    short-period part alone, 35-day high-pass  2.07 ms  = 0.96 m
    peak |dlod|                              0.93 ms/day

  The model is dominated by the 18.6-year nodal term, whose UT1 amplitude is
  0.162 s. The short-period figure is the part that separates UT1R from UT1
  under the conventional 35-day boundary. At the equator one millisecond of
  UT1 is 0.465 m of surface displacement.

  Handover approximation #6 previously quoted 30 microseconds and 14 mm for
  this quantity. Both are wrong by three to four orders of magnitude, in the
  direction that makes D30 more necessary rather than less. Corrected in
  revision 1.40 on the measurement above.

  The model's own accuracy is that of IERS Conventions (2010) Chapter 8; the
  coefficients are tabulated to four decimals in units of 1e-4 s, so the
  representation floor is about 10 ns in UT1.

ERRORS

referenceSystem:zonalTideEopCorrection:wrongScale
  referenceSystem:zonalTideEopCorrection:gatewayMissing

REFERENCES

IERS Conventions (2010), IERS Technical Note 36, Ch. 8, pp. 105-106.
  Yoder, C. F., Williams, J. G., Parke, M. E., 1981, J. Geophys. Res. 86, 881.
  Wahr, J. and Bergen, Z., 1986, Geophys. J. Roy. astr. Soc. 87, 633.
  Kantha, L. H., Stewart, J. S., Desai, S. D., 1998, J. Geophys. Res. 103, 12639.
  IERS Conventions software RG_ZONT2.F, published test case.

EXAMPLE

utc = referenceSystem.daySecondToEpoch([57754 0], "UTC");
  tt = referenceSystem.convertTimeScale(utc, "TT");
  correction = referenceSystem.zonalTideEopCorrection(tt);
  % Regularised UT1R, and only because it was asked for explicitly:
  e = referenceSystem.interpolateEop(eop, utc, ...
      'Correction', correction, 'Regularize', true);

KEYWORDS

EOP, zonal tide, UT1R, regularization, length of day, RG_ZONT2

See also INTERPOLATEEOP, SUBDAILYEOPCORRECTION, READEOP.

referenceSystem.downloadEop

Fetch a current IERS Earth orientation series, with local caching.

SYNTAX

file = downloadEop(series)
  file = downloadEop(series, 'CacheDir', folder)
  [file, info] = downloadEop(...)

  Downloads one of the series readEop understands and returns the path of the
  cached copy. This is the only function in the toolbox that touches the
  network, it is never required, and no test may depend on it.

INPUT ARGUMENTS

Name     Size   Type            Units  Description
  ----     ----   ----            -----  -----------
  series   1x1    string | char   -      "EOP 20 C04" (default source of
                                         record), "EOP 14 C04",
                                         "EOP 20 C04 dPsiDEpsilon" or
                                         "finals2000A".

OPTIONAL NAME-VALUE ARGUMENTS

Name       Size  Type      Default        Description
  ----       ----  ----      -------        -----------
  CacheDir   1x1   string    data/ under    Folder the copy is written to.
                             the toolbox    Created if absent.
  MaxAge     1x1   duration  days(7)        Reuse an existing cached file
                                            younger than this instead of
                                            fetching. Set to days(0) to force.
  Timeout    1x1   double    60             Seconds before the request fails.

OUTPUT ARGUMENTS

Name          Size   Type       Units  Description
  ----          ----   ----       -----  -----------
  file          1x1    string     -      Path of the cached series.
  info          1x1    struct     -      What happened. Fields below.
  .series       1x1    string     -      Series requested.
  .url          1x1    string     -      Source, or "" when the cache served.
  .fromCache    1x1    logical    -      True where nothing was fetched.
  .fileAge      1x1    duration   -      Age of the returned file.
  .bytes        1x1    double     -      Size of the returned file.
  .duration     1x1    double     s      Wall-clock time of the call.

METHOD

The toolbox must work on a machine with no network, so this function is
  strictly optional: nothing else calls it, no test requires it, and a user
  who supplies their own file never needs it. That constraint is why the
  download is a separate function rather than a fallback inside readEop, where
  it would make an offline session fail at an unpredictable moment.

  Caching is by file age rather than by content hash. The IERS updates these
  series daily, so an age test answers the question a user actually has (is
  this recent enough) at no cost, whereas a hash would require the download it
  is meant to avoid.

  The fetch is atomic in the sense that matters: the download goes to a
  temporary name in the cache folder and is moved into place only on success,
  so an interrupted transfer cannot leave a truncated series where a complete
  one was. A truncated EOP file is the worst possible failure here, because it
  parses, it validates, and it silently shortens the usable epoch range.

  The downloaded file is NOT parsed or validated here. Pass it to readEop,
  which owns format identification and validation; doing it in two places
  would be two sources of truth about what a valid series is.

ACCURACY

Not applicable: this function transfers bytes and computes nothing. The
  accuracy of the result is that of the series, which readEop records in
  eop.meta.

  One operational caveat that is an accuracy matter in disguise: the predicted
  part of finals2000A degrades with lead time, reaching tens of milliarcseconds
  in polar motion and milliseconds in UT1 a year ahead. Prefer a C04 series for
  anything reprocessed, and check eop.meta.series before quoting an accuracy.

ERRORS

referenceSystem:downloadEop:downloadFailed
  referenceSystem:downloadEop:cacheNotWritable

REFERENCES

IERS EOP Product Centre, https://hpiers.obspm.fr/eoppc/eop/
  IERS Rapid Service / Prediction Centre, https://maia.usno.navy.mil/

EXAMPLE

file = referenceSystem.downloadEop("EOP 20 C04");
  eop = referenceSystem.readEop(file);

KEYWORDS

EOP, download, cache, C04, finals2000A, IERS

See also READEOP, INTERPOLATEEOP.

L2 - Earth orientation transformation, ITRS <-> GCRS

referenceSystem.cipCoordinates

CIP coordinates and CIO locator, IAU 2006/2000A.

SYNTAX

[X, Y, s] = cipCoordinates(epoch)
  [X, Y, s] = cipCoordinates(epoch, 'Eop', eopAtEpoch)
  [X, Y, s] = cipCoordinates(epoch, 'DeltaX', dX, 'DeltaY', dY, ...
                             'CipModel', "IAU2006/2000A")

  Returns the coordinates X and Y of the Celestial Intermediate Pole in the
  GCRS and the CIO locator s, evaluated directly from the IAU 2006/2000A
  precession-nutation model and optionally corrected by the observed
  celestial pole offsets. The offsets are added in the frame they are
  referred to, which is the whole point of this function; see METHOD.

INPUT ARGUMENTS

Name    Size   Type     Units  Description
  ----    ----   ----     -----  -----------
  epoch   1x1    struct   -      Epoch struct, N entries, scale TT.

OPTIONAL NAME-VALUE ARGUMENTS

Name          Size        Type            Default  Description
  ----          ----        ----            -------  -----------
  Eop           1x1         struct          none     Output of
                                                     interpolateEop at these
                                                     epochs. Supplies dX, dY
                                                     and meta.cipModel in one
                                                     argument.
  DeltaX        1x1 | Nx1   double          0        Observed dX in radians,
                                                     when Eop is not given.
  DeltaY        1x1 | Nx1   double          0        Observed dY in radians.
  CipModel      1x1         string          from Eop Frame the offsets are
                                                     referred to, either
                                                     "IAU2006/2000A" or
                                                     "IAU2000A". Required
                                                     whenever an offset is
                                                     non-zero.
  Interpolant   1x1         struct          none     Reserved. Supplying it
                                                     raises notImplemented;
                                                     see METHOD.

OUTPUT ARGUMENTS

Name   Size   Type     Units  Description
  ----   ----   ----     -----  -----------
  X      Nx1    double   rad    CIP x-coordinate in the GCRS.
  Y      Nx1    double   rad    CIP y-coordinate in the GCRS.
  s      Nx1    double   rad    CIO locator, the position of the CIO on the
                                CIP equator relative to the point whose
                                direction is unchanged by precession-nutation.

METHOD

FINDING C-2, WHICH IS WHY THIS FUNCTION EXISTS SEPARATELY. The observed
  celestial pole offsets dX and dY are corrections to a model, and they are
  meaningless without knowing which model. v1 assumed IAU 2000A always, and
  therefore added the model bias (X00 - X06) at every epoch:

      v1:    X = X06 + (X00 - X06) + dX      unconditionally

  For a series whose offsets are already referred to IAU 2006/2000A that
  injects the bias as a spurious offset. Measured with the bundled SOFA
  release, (X00 - X06, Y00 - Y06) is (2.5, 39.1) microarcseconds at 2007 and
  (121.3, 134.7) at 2025, growing secularly. At 2025 that is 4.2 mm at the
  Earth's surface, which is exactly the level GRACE-like processing cares
  about. v1 also paid for a second full nutation series to compute it.

  Here the frame of the offsets is a property of the data:

      "IAU2006/2000A"   X = X06 + dX                iauXys00a never called
      "IAU2000A"        X = X06 + (X00 - X06) + dX  the bias is correct here

  and eop.meta.cipModel carries it from readEop, where it was recorded from
  the format registry or from the file. An unknown model is an error, not a
  default: the wrong choice produces a rotation that is orthogonal, smooth
  and plausible, and wrong by millimetres, which is a failure mode no runtime
  check downstream can catch.

  The decision itself lives in C, in rsCipAt of mex/rsCore.c, and is reached
  from here and from rotationItrsToGcrs alike (decision D61). Putting it in
  MATLAB would have meant either two implementations of one fix or six MEX
  round trips per single-epoch rotation.

  The model is evaluated DIRECTLY at every epoch, through SOFA iauXys06a.
  There is no interpolated path in v2.0 (decisions D17 and D24): interpolation
  belongs to earthOrientationInterpolant, which interpolates X, Y and s
  alongside the EOP quantities, and two mechanisms for one job would mean two
  error budgets and two code paths. 'Interpolant' is declared here so that the
  signature is final now and P19 changes no call site later.

  The vocabulary is direct and interpolated at every level of this toolbox,
  never exact and interp.

ACCURACY

Against the SOFA cookbook worked example of 2007-04-05 12:00:00 UTC
  (sofa_pn_c.pdf sections 5.3 and 5.5), measured, not asserted:

      X, offsets referred to IAU2006/2000A    8.99e-17 rad
      Y, offsets referred to IAU2006/2000A    2.88e-16 rad
      s                                       1.89e-11 arcsec
      X, offsets referred to IAU2000A         2.98e-17 rad
      Y, offsets referred to IAU2000A         1.24e-16 rad

  The two branches reach the same physical pole to 1.1e-13 rad, that is
  0.023 microarcseconds. That residual is the cookbook's own difference
  between its sections 5.3 and 5.5 and is the rounding of the published
  offsets to four decimals of a milliarcsecond, not an error here.

  The intrinsic evaluation noise of the series, measured as the residual of a
  degree-6 fit over a two-hour window where the model is smooth to far higher
  degree, is 4.5e-18 rad rms in X, 5.2e-17 in Y and 1.9e-20 in s. That is the
  floor of any finite difference built on this function and it is why
  rotationItrsToGcrs states a measured step rather than an assumed one.

  The physical accuracy of the model itself is that of IAU 2006/2000A, of
  order 0.2 milliarcseconds, which is four orders above everything above and
  is what the observed offsets exist to remove.

ERRORS

referenceSystem:cipCoordinates:wrongScale
  referenceSystem:cipCoordinates:unknownCipModel
  referenceSystem:cipCoordinates:cipModelRequired
  referenceSystem:cipCoordinates:sizeMismatch
  referenceSystem:cipCoordinates:conflictingOffsets
  referenceSystem:cipCoordinates:notImplemented
  referenceSystem:cipCoordinates:gatewayMissing

REFERENCES

IERS Conventions (2010), IERS Technical Note 36, Ch. 5.4 to 5.5.
  Wallace, P.T. and Capitaine, N., 2006, Astron. Astrophys. 459, 981
    (the IAU 2006/2000A CIP and the X, Y, s parameterisation).
  IAU SOFA release 20231011, iauXys06a, iauXys00a.
  SOFA Tools for Earth Attitude, sofa_pn_c.pdf, sections 5.3 and 5.5.

EXAMPLE

tt = referenceSystem.daySecondToEpoch([54195 43265.184], "TT");
  [X, Y, s] = referenceSystem.cipCoordinates(tt);
  assert(abs(X - 0.0007122638811749682) < 1e-15);

KEYWORDS

CIP, CIO, celestial intermediate pole, precession, nutation, dX, dY, SOFA

See also ROTATIONGCRSTOCIRS, ROTATIONITRSTOGCRS, INTERPOLATEEOP, READEOP.

referenceSystem.earthOrientationInterpolant

Chebyshev panels for the ITRS to GCRS chain.

SYNTAX

interpolant = earthOrientationInterpolant(epoch, 'Eop', eop) fits the six
  quantities the terrestrial-to-celestial rotation needs - the CIP X and Y,
  the CIO locator s, the two pole coordinates and UT1 minus UTC - over the
  span covered by epoch, and returns them as Chebyshev coefficients on
  consecutive panels.

  The result is accepted by rotationItrsToGcrs through its 'Interpolant'
  option, and it is the same object the public C API evaluates, so an
  integrator written in C can obtain the rotation with no MATLAB in its loop.

INPUT ARGUMENTS

Name    Size   Type     Units  Description
  ----    ----   ----     -----  -----------
  epoch   1x1    struct   -      Epoch struct, N entries, scale UTC. Only the
                                 earliest and latest entries are read: they
                                 bound the span to be covered. N = 2 is the
                                 usual call and N = 1 is refused, because a
                                 single instant does not describe a span.

OPTIONAL NAME-VALUE ARGUMENTS

Name        Size   Type     Default  Description
  ----        ----   ----     -------  -----------
  Eop         1x1    struct   none     Series from readEop. Required: the
                                       pole and UT1 are measured and cannot
                                       be modelled.
  Degree      1x1    double   20       Chebyshev degree per panel. The
                                       default is decision D82 and is
                                       measured, not assumed - see METHOD.
  PanelSpan   1x1    double   1        Panel length in days.
  ApplyTidalCorrections
              1x1    logical  true     Whether the sub-daily ocean tide and
                                       libration are folded into the fitted
                                       pole and UT1. MUST MATCH the setting
                                       the consumer uses; an interpolant
                                       built without them is wrong by tens of
                                       microarcseconds for a caller that
                                       wants them, which is why the choice is
                                       recorded in .meta rather than assumed.
  Tolerance   1x1    double   0.5      Residual the fit must achieve, in
                                       microarcseconds. Checked against
                                       samples the fit has NOT seen. A fit
                                       that misses it raises toleranceNotMet
                                       rather than returning quietly.

OUTPUT ARGUMENTS

Name              Size          Type     Units  Description
  ----              ----          ----     -----  -----------
  interpolant       1x1           struct   -      The panels. Fields below.
  .panelStartMjd    Px1           double   d      UTC MJD at which each panel
                                                  begins. Consecutive and
                                                  equally spaced.
  .panelSpan        1x1           double   d      Panel length.
  .degree           1x1           double   -      Chebyshev degree.
  .taiMinusUtc      Px1           double   s      Leap second count on each
                                                  panel. The public C
                                                  interface needs it to reach
                                                  TT from UTC without a leap
                                                  second table of its own, and
                                                  a per-panel scalar is EXACT
                                                  rather than approximate
                                                  because panels are aligned
                                                  to whole UTC days, so a leap
                                                  second falls on a boundary.
  .quantity         1x7           string   -      Column order of the
                                                  coefficients. THE SINGLE
                                                  AUTHORITY for that order;
                                                  no caller may assume it.
  .coefficient      (D+1)x6xP     double   mixed  Chebyshev coefficients,
                                                  D = degree. Units follow
                                                  .quantity: radians for the
                                                  first five, seconds for
                                                  dut1.
  .meta             1x1           struct   -      Provenance. Fields below.
  .meta.residual    1x6           double   uas    Worst error against the
                                                  direct chain at samples the
                                                  fit did not see. The dut1
                                                  entry is microseconds, not
                                                  microarcseconds.
  .meta.tolerance   1x1           double   uas    Tolerance that was met.
  .meta.cipModel    1x1           string   -      Frame of the CIP offsets.
  .meta.eopFile     1x1           string   -      Series the fit was built on.
  .meta.spanMjd     1x2           double   d      Span actually covered,
                                                  which is the requested span
                                                  rounded outward to whole
                                                  panels.
  .meta.tidalCorrections
                    1x1           logical  -      Whether the sub-daily terms
                                                  are in the fit.
  .meta.builtOn     1x1           string   -      Toolbox version and date.

METHOD

One panel per PanelSpan days, aligned to whole days so that panel
  boundaries fall where the EOP series changes row. Inside a panel each
  quantity is a Chebyshev expansion on Gauss-Lobatto nodes.

  THE FIT IS NOT PERFORMED HERE. The nodes, the fit and the evaluation are
  the C routines in mex/rsCheb.c, reached through the gateway. A second
  implementation in MATLAB would be a second authority for the same
  arithmetic, and the MATLAB path and the C API would then agree only by
  inspection. They now share one fit by construction.

  DEGREE 20 OVER ONE DAY IS MEASURED, NOT PROPOSED. Handover section B.9.2
  records the sweep. The binding quantity is not the CIP, which reaches its
  double-precision floor at degree 8 over a day and does not improve after,
  but the sub-daily ocean tide and libration, whose semi-diurnal terms need
  degree 20 to reach 2.6e-7 uas. Degree 16 reaches 3.6e-4 uas and degree 12
  only 0.2 uas. Risk R5 proposed 300 s node spacing from rev. 1.0; degree 20
  over a day is an effective 4320 s and lands four orders below the tightest
  tolerance this interface offers, so the proposal was over-conservative by
  about fifteen and was aimed at the wrong quantity.

  THE RESIDUAL IS COMPUTED AT POINTS THE FIT HAS NOT SEEN. Evaluating at the
  nodes returns the node values to roundoff by construction and would report
  a residual of zero for any degree, which is worse than reporting none. The
  check therefore samples the midpoints between nodes, where a Chebyshev fit
  is at its weakest, and compares against the same direct chain
  rotationItrsToGcrs would have evaluated.

ACCURACY

With the default degree and panel span, against the direct chain:
    CIP X, Y, s              better than 1e-4 uas
    pole xp, yp              better than 1e-3 uas
    UT1 - UTC                better than 1e-4 us
  These are fit errors only. They do not improve on the EOP series itself,
  whose own formal errors are tens of microarcseconds, and they are not a
  claim about the accuracy of the rotation - only that interpolating it costs
  nothing measurable against evaluating it directly.

  The interpolant is C-infinity inside a panel and continuous but not
  differentiable across a panel boundary. That is still an improvement on the
  direct path, whose piecewise linear EOP interpolation puts a slope
  discontinuity at every midnight into a variable-step integrator's right
  hand side.

ERRORS

referenceSystem:earthOrientationInterpolant:wrongScale
  referenceSystem:earthOrientationInterpolant:singleEpoch
  referenceSystem:earthOrientationInterpolant:spanNotCovered
  referenceSystem:earthOrientationInterpolant:toleranceNotMet

REFERENCES

IERS Conventions (2010), IERS Technical Note 36, chapter 5.
  Handover section B.9.1 and B.9.2, decisions D22 and D82, risk R5.

EXAMPLE

eop = referenceSystem.readEop('eop20c04.txt');
  span = referenceSystem.daySecondToEpoch([57754 0; 57764 0], "UTC");
  ip = referenceSystem.earthOrientationInterpolant(span, 'Eop', eop);
  R = referenceSystem.rotationItrsToGcrs(epoch, 'Interpolant', ip);

KEYWORDS

Chebyshev, interpolation, Earth orientation, CIP, polar motion, UT1,
  precise orbit determination

See also rotationItrsToGcrs, cipCoordinates, interpolateEop, readEop

Developed by Matthias Weigelt with the assistance of Claude Opus 5 (Anthropic)
Created: 2026-07-27 10:20 (CEST, UTC+02:00)

referenceSystem.earthOrientationPartials

Partials of the ITRS to GCRS rotation in the EOP.

SYNTAX

[dXp, dYp, dUt1] = earthOrientationPartials(epoch, 'Eop', eop) returns the
  partial derivatives of the terrestrial-to-celestial rotation with respect to
  the two pole coordinates and to UT1 minus UTC.

  These are the columns a least-squares adjustment needs when the Earth
  orientation parameters are estimated rather than held fixed - in precise
  orbit determination, in terrestrial frame realisation, and wherever an
  observation residual has to be differentiated with respect to the pole.

INPUT ARGUMENTS

Name    Size   Type     Units  Description
  ----    ----   ----     -----  -----------
  epoch   1x1    struct   -      Epoch struct, N entries, any scale.

OPTIONAL NAME-VALUE ARGUMENTS

Name          Size   Type     Default  Description
  ----          ----   ----     -------  -----------
  Eop           1x1    struct   none     Series from readEop, as
                                         rotationItrsToGcrs takes it.
  Interpolant   1x1    struct   []       Panels from
                                         earthOrientationInterpolant.
  Rotation      3x3xN   double  []       A rotation already computed for
                                         these epochs. Supply it and the
                                         chain is not evaluated again; the
                                         partials are three matrix products.

OUTPUT ARGUMENTS

Name              Size     Type     Units    Description
  ----              ----     ----     -----    -----------
  partialXp         3x3xN    double   1/rad    dR/dxp.
  partialYp         3x3xN    double   1/rad    dR/dyp.
  partialDeltaUt1   3x3xN    double   1/s      dR/d(UT1-UTC).
  info              1x1      struct   -        .generatorAxis, the three
                                               terrestrial axes used, as a
                                               3x3 whose columns correspond
                                               to the three outputs; and
                                               .rotationSupplied, whether the
                                               caller provided R.

METHOD

Every one of the three has the same shape:

      dR/dp = R * skew(a_p)

  with a_p a CONSTANT axis in the terrestrial frame. That is not an
  approximation of convenience; it follows from each parameter entering the
  product W*R3(theta)*Q through exactly one factor, and from the identity
  skew(R*v) = R*skew(v)*R'. The axes are

      xp        a = ( 0, -1,  0)
      yp        a = (-1,  0,  0)
      UT1-UTC   a = ( 0,  0,  omega)      omega from earthRotationAngleRate

  xp ROTATES ABOUT MINUS Y AND yp ABOUT MINUS X, not the other way round.
  iauPom00 builds W as R3(-s')*R2(xp)*R1(yp), so xp enters through the
  SECOND-axis factor and yp through the first. Revision 1.107 had them
  transposed - the natural reading of "the x pole coordinate" is a rotation
  about x, and it is wrong. The finite-difference test caught it on the first
  run, which is the only reason this comment can be written in the past
  tense.

  THE SIGNS WERE MEASURED, NOT DERIVED FROM THE FACTOR ORDER OF iauPom00.
  Handover revision 1.106 records the experiment: the generators were
  finite-differenced against rsRotationItrsToGcrs itself, so the code being
  differentiated is the authority for its own derivative. Differentiating a
  remembered factor order is the one place in this function where a sign error
  is likely, and a sign error in a partial does not announce itself - it slows
  a least-squares convergence or biases an estimate, and it looks like a
  modelling problem rather than a defect. The measurement also produced a free
  check: all three generators came out SKEW-SYMMETRIC, which the generator of
  a rotation must be and which a finite difference has no way to satisfy by
  accident.

ACCURACY

The constant-axis form neglects terms of order xp, yp and s', which are
  below 1e-6 radians, so the partials are exact to about one part in 1e6 of
  themselves. That is six orders better than any use a partial is put to: a
  normal matrix built from these is limited by the observation model, never by
  this. The exact form would carry the CIP unit vector and the pole through
  each generator; it is not used because it would cost a second evaluation of
  the chain to buy accuracy nothing needs.

  The partials are exact derivatives of the DIRECT path. If an interpolant is
  supplied, they are the derivatives of the interpolated rotation, which
  differs from the direct one by the fit residual in interpolant.meta.

ERRORS

referenceSystem:earthOrientationPartials:sizeMismatch

REFERENCES

IERS Conventions (2010), IERS Technical Note 36, chapter 5.
  Handover revision 1.106 for the measured generators.

EXAMPLE

eop = referenceSystem.readEop('eop20c04.txt');
  utc = referenceSystem.daySecondToEpoch([57754 0], "UTC");
  [dXp, dYp, dUt1] = referenceSystem.earthOrientationPartials(utc, 'Eop', eop);

KEYWORDS

partial derivative, Earth orientation, polar motion, UT1, least squares,
  precise orbit determination

See also rotationItrsToGcrs, rotationItrsToGcrsPartials,
  earthRotationAngleRate, interpolateEop

referenceSystem.rotationItrsToGcrsPartials

Partials of the ITRS-to-GCRS rotation w.r.t. EOP.

SYNTAX

[dRdXp, dRdYp, dRdDeltaUt1] = rotationItrsToGcrsPartials(epoch, Eop = eop)
  [..., info] = rotationItrsToGcrsPartials(...)
  [..., info, dRdotdXp, dRdotdYp, dRdotdDeltaUt1] = rotationItrsToGcrsPartials(...)

  For estimating Earth orientation parameters alongside an orbit, or for
  propagating their formal errors into a state. All three rotation partials
  are ANALYTIC and cost about as much as one extra matrix product each.

INPUT ARGUMENTS

Name    Size   Type     Units  Description
  ----    ----   ----     -----  -----------
  epoch   1x1    struct   -      Epoch struct, N entries, UTC or convertible.

OPTIONAL NAME-VALUE ARGUMENTS

Name              Size   Type      Default  Description
  ----              ----   ----      -------  -----------
  Eop               1x1    struct    none     Series from readEop. Required.
  CipModel          1x1    string    ""       Frame of the pole offsets:
                                              "IAU2006/2000A" or "IAU2000A".
                                              Empty takes it from the series'
                                              own metadata, which is the right
                                              default because the series
                                              declares its frame.
  DifferenceStep    1x1    double    200      Half-offset h of the central
                                              difference in seconds, for the
                                              RATE partials only: they sample
                                              at t-h and t+h and divide by
                                              2h, EXACTLY the meaning the
                                              same option has in
                                              rotationItrsToGcrs (D112).
                                              Ignored when the rate partials
                                              are not requested.
  ApplyTidalCorrections 1x1 logical  true     Sub-daily EOP corrections.

OUTPUT ARGUMENTS

Name              Size    Type     Units      Description
  ----              ----    ----     -----      -----------
  dRdXp             3x3xN   double   1/rad      d R / d xp.
  dRdYp             3x3xN   double   1/rad      d R / d yp.
  dRdDeltaUt1       3x3xN   double   1/s        d R / d Delta UT1.
  info              1x1     struct   -          Fields listed below.
  dRdotdXp          3x3xN   double   1/(rad s)  d Rdot / d xp.
  dRdotdYp          3x3xN   double   1/(rad s)  d Rdot / d yp.
  dRdotdDeltaUt1    3x3xN   double   1/s^2      d Rdot / d Delta UT1.
  .cipModel         1x1     string   -          Model actually used.
  .differenceStep   1x1     double   s          Step used for the rate
                                                partials, NaN if not asked for.
  .rateConstruction 1x1     string   -          How the rate partials were
                                                formed. See METHOD.
  .polarMotionUnits 1x1     string   -          "radian", stated because the
                                                series is published in arcsec.

  OUTPUTS ARE nargout-GATED AND ORDERED BY COST (design principle 15). The
  three rotation partials are analytic and nearly free, info is free, and the
  three rate partials each need two more evaluations - so asking for FIVE
  outputs is where the cost changes.

  info SITS FOURTH AND NOT LAST, WHICH DEPARTS FROM rotationItrsToGcrs ON
  PURPOSE. There, the outputs are one series of increasing derivative order
  and info is genuinely last and free. Here there are two GROUPS, and burying
  info behind the expensive one makes the cheap path unable to report itself:
  with info seventh, asking for provenance would trip the gate and compute
  all three rate partials, and info.differenceStep = NaN - the value that
  says no rate was computed - would be unreachable. Principle 15 orders by
  increasing cost, and info costs nothing.

METHOD

The rotation is assembled as R = (W Z Q)' - polar motion, then Earth
  rotation, then precession-nutation, transposed because rsCore stores the
  celestial-to-terrestrial product. Each EOP enters exactly one factor, which
  is why the partials are analytic and cheap:

    W = Rx(-yp) Ry(-xp) Rz(s')        [SOFA iauPom00, read from pom00.c]
    Z = Rz(ERA)
    Q = C2I(X, Y, s)

    dR/dxp        = ( Rx(-yp) * (-Ry'(-xp)) * Rz(s') * Z * Q )'
    dR/dyp        = ( (-Rx'(-yp)) * Ry(-xp) * Rz(s') * Z * Q )'
    dR/dDeltaUT1  = ( W * Z'(ERA) * omega_ERA * Q )'

  The minus signs are not decoration: iauPom00 applies Ry and Rx with NEGATED
  arguments, so the chain rule contributes a factor of -1 to each of the two
  polar-motion partials and to neither of the others.

  omega_ERA IS iersConstants().eraRatePerUt1Second AND NOT earthRotationRate.
  The two differ by 2.0e-8 relative, and earthRotationRate's own help forbids
  using it to propagate an angle: it is a nominal mean rate, while ERA is
  exactly linear in UT1 with the slope 2*pi*1.00273781191135448/86400. Using
  the wrong one biases every Delta UT1 partial by 2e-8, systematically and
  invisibly. Length of day does not enter: LOD relates UT1 to TAI, not ERA
  to UT1.

  THE RATE PARTIALS ARE THE DIFFERENCE OF TWO ANALYTIC PARTIALS (decision
  D102), not the derivative of a differenced quantity:

    dRdot/dp  =  [ dR/dp (t+h) - dR/dp (t-h) ] / (2h),   h = DifferenceStep

  Differentiate with respect to the parameter first, difference in time
  second. h here IS the h of rotationItrsToGcrs's Rdot under D28 - the
  half-offset, samples at t-h and t+h, divisor 2h - and the default 200 s is
  that function's measured default, so with the defaults the rate partial is
  the partial of the Rdot the rotation actually returns (decision D112).
  Until rev. 1.184 this function read DifferenceStep as the FULL span,
  sampling at t-h/2 with a default of 600 s; the same option name carried
  two meanings in two sibling functions, which is the class of silent
  defect O28 exists for, and the same-step claim above was false as
  written. The other order - differentiating the already-differenced Rdot -
  would agree only to the differencing error and would drift from Rdot as
  the step changed. info.rateConstruction records which was done, because
  the two are not interchangeable and a reader cannot tell from the numbers.

ACCURACY

The rotation partials are exact to roundoff: they are closed-form products
  of the same factors the rotation itself is built from, with one elementary
  rotation replaced by its derivative. Verified against a central difference
  of rotationItrsToGcrs with a perturbed EOP value; the derivation was
  checked in an independent implementation before this file was written, with
  residuals of 5.2e-11, 5.1e-11 and 4.0e-13 at the difference's own roundoff
  floor.

  THE RATE PARTIALS CARRY THE DIFFERENCING ERROR AND NOTHING BETTER. They are
  O(h^2) accurate like Rdot, and the default h = 200 s is the measured D28
  optimum rotationItrsToGcrs uses for Rdot itself; a smaller step trades
  truncation for cancellation and the convergence block in the test report
  shows where the floor is.

  THERE ARE NO PARTIALS OF Rddot, AND THAT IS A DECISION (D105, open item
  B2). P20 delivers dR/dp and dRdot/dp; the second derivative is untouched.
  Rddot exists because Qddot reaches 1.4e-10 m/s^2, the GRACE accelerometer
  noise floor, which is a FORCE-MODEL argument: it matters to an integrator
  propagating a state, not to an estimator solving for EOP. A normal matrix
  is built from partials of the OBSERVED quantities, and no observation in
  this toolbox's scope is a function of Rddot. Adding them would cost two
  further evaluations per epoch, following D102's ordering a second time, to
  populate a block nothing multiplies. Recorded rather than left ambiguous:
  the question was asked and answered, not overlooked. If an estimator ever
  does need them, the construction is the one already used here and the
  decision is cheap to reverse.

  THE PARTIALS ARE PER RADIAN, NOT PER ARCSECOND. Polar motion is published
  in arcseconds and Delta UT1 in seconds; xp and yp reach this function in
  RADIANS, as everywhere else in the toolbox, so a caller building a normal
  matrix from an arcsecond-parameterised estimate must scale by
  iersConstants().arcsecondsToRadians. info.polarMotionUnits says so on every
  call, because a factor of 206265 in a partial is not visible in the answer.

ERRORS

referenceSystem:rotationItrsToGcrsPartials:eopRequired
  referenceSystem:rotationItrsToGcrsPartials:gatewayMissing

REFERENCES

IERS Conventions (2010), IERS Technical Note 36, Ch. 5.
  IAU SOFA release 20231011, iauPom00, iauC2ixys, iauEra00, iauSp00.
  Handover decisions D9 (EOP estimation in scope), D28, D102.

EXAMPLE

eop = referenceSystem.readEop("eopc04_20.1962-now");
  t   = referenceSystem.daySecondToEpoch([57754 43200], "UTC");
  [dXp, dYp, dUt1] = referenceSystem.rotationItrsToGcrsPartials(t, Eop = eop);

KEYWORDS

partial derivative, EOP, polar motion, Delta UT1, estimation, POD, Jacobian

See also ROTATIONITRSTOGCRS, EARTHORIENTATIONPARTIALS, INTERPOLATEEOP,
  IERSCONSTANTS.

referenceSystem.evaluateInterpolant

Evaluate one panel of an Earth orientation interpolant.

SYNTAX

value = evaluateInterpolant(interpolant, mjd, panelIndex) returns the six
  quantities named by interpolant.quantity at the given UTC MJD, read from
  the panel at panelIndex.

  This is the low-level reader. rotationItrsToGcrs will select the panel and
  assemble the rotation itself; until that option is wired this is how the
  panels are reached, and it is what the C API exposes.

INPUT ARGUMENTS

Name          Size   Type     Units  Description
  ----          ----   ----     -----  -----------
  interpolant   1x1    struct   -      Output of earthOrientationInterpolant.
  mjd           Nx1    double   d      UTC Modified Julian Dates, all inside
                                       the named panel.
  panelIndex    1x1    double   -      Index into interpolant.panelStartMjd.

OUTPUT ARGUMENTS

Name    Size   Type     Units  Description
  ----    ----   ----     -----  -----------
  value   Nx6    double   mixed  The six quantities in the order given by
                                 interpolant.quantity: radians for the first
                                 five, seconds for dut1.

METHOD

Clenshaw evaluation in mex/rsCheb.c, reached through the gateway, so this
  function and the public C API share one evaluator rather than agreeing by
  inspection. The panel is not searched for here: the caller names it,
  because in an integrator hot loop the panel changes once per day and
  searching for it at every step would cost more than the evaluation.

ACCURACY

Exact to roundoff against the coefficients it is given. The accuracy of
  those coefficients against the direct chain is earthOrientationInterpolant's
  claim and is recorded in interpolant.meta.residual.

ERRORS

referenceSystem:evaluateInterpolant:badPanel
  referenceSystem:mex:outOfPanel

REFERENCES

Handover sections B.9.1 and B.9.2, decision D82.

EXAMPLE

eop = referenceSystem.readEop('eop20c04.txt');
  span = referenceSystem.daySecondToEpoch([57754 0; 57755 0], "UTC");
  ip = referenceSystem.earthOrientationInterpolant(span, 'Eop', eop);
  value = referenceSystem.evaluateInterpolant(ip, 57754.5, 1);

KEYWORDS

Chebyshev, evaluation, Earth orientation, interpolation

See also earthOrientationInterpolant, rotationItrsToGcrs

referenceSystem.writeInterpolant

Write an Earth orientation interpolant to a portable file.

SYNTAX

file = writeInterpolant(interpolant, file) writes the panels produced by
  earthOrientationInterpolant in the format mex/rsApi.h reads, so an orbit
  integrator written in C can obtain the rotation with no MATLAB in its loop.

INPUT ARGUMENTS

Name          Size   Type     Units  Description
  ----          ----   ----     -----  -----------
  interpolant   1x1    struct   -      Output of earthOrientationInterpolant.
  file          1x1    string   -      Destination path.

OUTPUT ARGUMENTS

Name   Size   Type     Units  Description
  ----   ----   ----     -----  -----------
  file   1x1    string   -      The path written, for chaining.

METHOD

TEXT, NOT BINARY, AND THE COEFFICIENTS CARRY %.17g. Seventeen significant
  decimal digits round-trip an IEEE-754 double EXACTLY, so nothing is lost
  while the file stays inspectable, diffable, and free of endianness and
  struct padding. A binary format would be shorter and would make a byte-order
  defect look like an accuracy problem in an integrator days later, which is
  the characteristic failure of this domain.

  Coefficients are panel-major and quantity-major within a panel, the layout
  rsApi.h documents: one panel is contiguous, because an integrator stays
  inside one for a whole day.

  ONE VALUE PER LINE. Wider rows would be shorter, but a reader that lost
  count would resynchronise silently on the next line; one per line makes a
  truncated file a parse failure rather than a shifted array.

  The tidal setting is written because A READER MUST BE ABLE TO REFUSE A
  MISMATCH: an interpolant built without the sub-daily terms differs by tens
  of microarcseconds and the resulting rotation gives no sign of it (rev.
  1.91).

ACCURACY

Exact. Every coefficient survives the round trip bit for bit, and the test
  asserts it with AbsTol 0 rather than a tolerance.

ERRORS

referenceSystem:writeInterpolant:badInterpolant
  referenceSystem:writeInterpolant:cannotOpen

REFERENCES

mex/rsApi.h for the same layout on the C side; handover D82.

EXAMPLE

eop = referenceSystem.readEop('eop20c04.txt');
  span = referenceSystem.daySecondToEpoch([57754 0; 57757 0], "UTC");
  ip = referenceSystem.earthOrientationInterpolant(span, 'Eop', eop);
  referenceSystem.writeInterpolant(ip, "arc.eopi");

KEYWORDS

interpolant, serialisation, Chebyshev, C interface, Earth orientation

See also earthOrientationInterpolant, readInterpolant, evaluateInterpolant

-------------------------------------------------------------------------
referenceSystem v2.1
Developed by Matthias Weigelt with the assistance of Claude Opus 5 (Anthropic)
Created: 2026-07-27 21:10 (CEST, UTC+02:00)
-------------------------------------------------------------------------

back to contents

referenceSystem.readInterpolant

Read an interpolant written by writeInterpolant.

SYNTAX

interpolant = readInterpolant(file) reconstructs the struct
  earthOrientationInterpolant produced, so a file written on one machine can
  be used on another and so the round trip is testable from MATLAB.

INPUT ARGUMENTS

Name   Size   Type     Units  Description
  ----   ----   ----     -----  -----------
  file   1x1    string   -      Path written by writeInterpolant.

OUTPUT ARGUMENTS

Name          Size   Type     Units  Description
  ----          ----   ----     -----  -----------
  interpolant   1x1    struct   -      As earthOrientationInterpolant returns
                                       it, except that .meta carries only
                                       what the file holds: cipModel and
                                       tidalCorrections.

METHOD

Header keys are read BY NAME, not by position, so a field added to the
  writer does not shift everything after it.

  AN UNKNOWN VERSION IS REFUSED rather than read hopefully. A format that
  changed and a reader that guessed would produce coefficients that are
  plausible and wrong, which is how every silent failure in this project has
  presented itself.

  THE RESIDUAL IS NOT CARRIED. earthOrientationInterpolant measures it
  against samples the fit did not see; a number copied through a file is no
  longer a measurement of anything. A caller who needs it rebuilds.

ACCURACY

Exact. Every coefficient is recovered bit for bit from the %.17g written.

ERRORS

referenceSystem:readInterpolant:cannotOpen
  referenceSystem:readInterpolant:badFormat

REFERENCES

mex/rsApi.h for the same layout on the C side.

EXAMPLE

ip = referenceSystem.readInterpolant("arc.eopi");   % written earlier by
  value = referenceSystem.evaluateInterpolant(ip, ...   % writeInterpolant
      ip.panelStartMjd(1) + 0.5, 1);

KEYWORDS

interpolant, deserialisation, Chebyshev, Earth orientation

See also writeInterpolant, earthOrientationInterpolant

referenceSystem.earthRotationAngleRate

Rate of advance of the Earth rotation angle.

SYNTAX

rate = earthRotationAngleRate()

  Returns d(ERA)/dt in radians per second: the constant of the IAU 2000
  definition of the Earth rotation angle, uncorrected for the excess length
  of day. It is the single authority for that constant in this toolbox.

INPUT ARGUMENTS

none.

OUTPUT ARGUMENTS

Name   Size   Type     Units    Description
  ----   ----   ----     -----    -----------
  rate   1x1    double   rad/s    d(ERA)/dt, nominal.

METHOD

Not a literal. ERA is linear in UT1 by definition,

      ERA(Tu) = 2*pi * (0.7790572732640 + 1.00273781191135448 * Tu)

  so its rate is obtained exactly by differencing SOFA iauEra00 over a
  1000 s interval of UT1 and dividing. That keeps the bundled SOFA release
  as the one authority for the defining constant, in the same way that
  leapSecondTable derives its table from iauDat instead of storing one: a
  SOFA update propagates with no MATLAB edit, and there is no second copy to
  drift.

  The value is cached in a persistent variable, so the two gateway calls
  happen once per MATLAB session and never inside a hot loop. Clearing the
  function discards the cache.

  THIS FUNCTION EXISTS BECAUSE THE OBVIOUS CONSTANT IS THE WRONG ONE
  (decision D63). iersConstants.earthRotationRate is 7.292115e-5 rad/s, the
  nominal mean angular velocity of the Earth from IERS Conventions Table 1.1.
  The rate of advance of ERA is 7.2921151467e-5 rad/s. The relative
  difference is 2.0e-8, which reaches 10.3 micrometres per second of
  co-rotation velocity at a 7000 km orbit radius, above the length-of-day
  term and two hundred times the polar-motion rate term of the same
  derivative. Without this function the two constants would be one call site
  apart and indistinguishable by inspection, which is finding M-2 in a new
  costume.

  Interval choice. 1000 s advances ERA by 0.073 rad, large enough that the
  difference of two angles of order unity keeps 14 significant digits, and
  small enough that no wrap of iauEra00 into [0, 2*pi) can fall inside it.

ACCURACY

Measured 7.292115146705669e-5 rad/s against the exact defining value
  7.292115146706979e-5, a difference of 1.3e-18 rad/s carried entirely by the
  differencing interval. At 7000 km that is 9 picometres per second.

ERRORS

referenceSystem:earthRotationAngleRate:gatewayMissing

REFERENCES

IERS Conventions (2010), IERS Technical Note 36, Ch. 5.5.3, Eq. 5.15.
  Capitaine, N., Guinot, B. and McCarthy, D.D., 2000, Astron. Astrophys.
    355, 398.
  IAU SOFA release 20231011, iauEra00.

EXAMPLE

omega = referenceSystem.earthRotationAngleRate();
  assert(abs(omega - 7.2921151467e-5) < 1e-15);

KEYWORDS

ERA, Earth rotation rate, omega, UT1, sidereal, constant, SOFA

See also EARTHROTATIONANGLE, IERSCONSTANTS, ROTATIONITRSTOGCRS.

Developed by Matthias Weigelt with the assistance of Claude Opus 5 (Anthropic)
