referenceSystem.integrateAttitudeIncrements

Propagate attitude over integrated angle increments.

SYNTAX

q = integrateAttitudeIncrements(initialQuaternion, deltaTheta, epoch)
  propagates the initial attitude through a series of INTEGRATED angle
  increments of the body frame relative to the navigation frame, resolved in
  the BODY frame, and returns the attitude at each of the N epochs. Increment
  k spans epoch k to epoch k+1, so deltaTheta holds N-1 columns.

  [q, info] = integrateAttitudeIncrements(...) also returns what the
  propagation did, including whether the first interval carried a coning
  correction, which it cannot.

INPUT ARGUMENTS

Name                Size      Type     Units  Description
  ----                ----      ----     -----  -----------
  initialQuaternion   4x1       double   -      Attitude at the FIRST epoch,
                                                rows ordered by the
                                                convention. Need not be of
                                                unit norm.
  deltaTheta          3x(N-1)   double   rad    Integral of the body angular
                                                velocity over each interval,
                                                body resolved. Column k spans
                                                epoch k to epoch k+1.
  epoch               1x1       struct   -      Epoch struct holding N
                                                strictly increasing epochs.
                                                The spacing need not be
                                                uniform.

OPTIONAL NAME-VALUE ARGUMENTS

Name              Size   Type     Default                Description
  ----              ----   ----     -------                -----------
  Convention        1x1    struct   attitudeConvention()   .quaternion is read.
  ConingCorrection  1x1    logical  true                   Include the coning
                                                           term. False is for
                                                           demonstrating its
                                                           effect, not for use.
  RenormalizeEvery  1x1    double   1                      Steps between
                                                           renormalisations.

OUTPUT ARGUMENTS

Name                       Size   Type      Units  Description
  ----                       ----   ----      -----  -----------
  q                          4xN    double    -      Attitude at each epoch,
                                                     column one being the
                                                     initial attitude
                                                     renormalised. SIGN
                                                     CONTINUOUS.
  info                       1x1    struct    -      Diagnostics.
  .method                    1x1    string    -      "rotationVector".
  .stepCount                 1x1    double    -      N-1.
  .totalAngle                1x1    double    rad    Accumulated rotation
                                                     angle.
  .largestStepAngle          1x1    double    rad    Largest single step.
  .largestConingAngle        1x1    double    rad    Largest coning term
                                                     applied, zero when it is
                                                     switched off.
  .coningCorrection          1x1    logical   -      Whether it was applied.
  .firstIntervalUncorrected  1x1    logical   -      True whenever the coning
                                                     correction is on, since
                                                     the first interval has
                                                     no predecessor.
  .renormalizeEvery          1x1    double    -      The cadence used.
  convention                 1x1    struct    -      The convention used.

METHOD

THIS IS NOT integrateAttitude WITH A DIFFERENT UNIT ON ITS SECOND ARGUMENT,
  AND THAT IS WHY IT IS A SEPARATE FUNCTION. Section C.7 rule 2 keeps meaning
  in the name rather than in a flag; an argument whose UNITS depend on an
  option is the decision D25 and risk R10 failure mode in a new place, where
  rad and rad/s differ by a factor no validator could catch. The coning term
  also has a different form here, so the two are not one algorithm with a
  switch.

  THE ROTATION VECTOR IS NOT THE INTEGRAL OF THE ANGULAR VELOCITY, and the
  difference is the whole of the correction. Bortz's equation for the
  rotation vector phi of an interval is

      phi_dot = w + 0.5*cross(phi, w) + (1/12)*cross(phi, cross(phi, w)) + ...

  so to second order phi(T) = alpha(T) + 0.5*integral(cross(alpha, w) dt),
  with alpha the accumulated increment inside the interval. Model w as linear
  across the previous and current intervals, of lengths Tp and Tc. The mean
  rate over an interval is then w at its midpoint, which fixes the slope, and
  evaluating the integral gives

      phi_k = dTheta_k + Tc^2/(6*Tp*(Tp + Tc)) * cross(dTheta_(k-1), dTheta_k)

  DERIVED HERE, NOT QUOTED. On a uniform grid the weight is exactly 1/12,
  which is the classical previous-sample coning algorithm; the general form
  is carried because a real gyro grid is not uniform. GRACE-FO IMU1B jitters
  by +-3.9 ms about 0.125 s, and assuming 1/12 there is a 3 per cent error on
  a term that is itself small — but the general form costs two divisions and
  removes the question.

  THE FIRST INTERVAL CARRIES NO CORRECTION AND info SAYS SO. No increment
  precedes it, so no single-sample algorithm can correct it. The cost is one
  interval's worth of coning error, once, and it does not accumulate; it is
  reported rather than hidden because a caller splitting a long arc into
  pieces pays it once per piece and should know to overlap instead.

  THE COMPOSITION IS NOT DUPLICATED. Once the rotation vectors exist, this
  function hands them to the package-private composeRotationVectors, the same
  authority integrateAttitude uses. The exponential map and the Hamilton
  product exist once in this toolbox.

  THERE IS NO Method OPTION. Runge-Kutta integrates the quaternion
  differential equation and needs a RATE; recovering one by dividing the
  increments by their intervals would throw away exactly the information that
  makes this function worth having. Offering it would be an option that
  silently undoes the point of the function.

ACCURACY

MEASURED AGAINST THE CLOSED-FORM CONING CASE, WHERE THE INCREMENTS, THE
  RATE AND THE ATTITUDE ALL HAVE ANALYTIC FORMS, so nothing below is produced
  by the code under test. Half-cone 3 degrees, cone rate pi/2 rad/s, arc 2 s:

      dt [s]    this function    integrateAttitude    ratio
      0.0200    7.22e-09 rad     8.63e-06 rad         1194x
      0.0100    8.95e-10 rad     2.16e-06 rad         2411x
      0.0050    1.11e-10 rad     5.39e-07 rad         4845x
      0.0025    1.39e-11 rad     1.35e-07 rad         9714x

  THE RATIO GROWS BECAUSE THE TWO ARE OF DIFFERENT ORDER, not because one is
  a better version of the other: this function is O(dt^3) and the rate form
  is O(dt^2). The rate form is limited by reconstructing omega between two
  samples, which no attitude integrator can repair; an exact increment does
  not need reconstructing.

  THE CONING TERM EARNS ITS PLACE HERE FAR MORE CLEARLY THAN IT DOES ON
  RATES. Switching it off costs a factor of 98 at dt = 0.02 s and 797 at
  dt = 0.0025 s, against a factor of 2 for the rate form, where the
  trapezoidal reconstruction of omega dominates and masks it.

  THOSE FIGURES ASSUME EXACT INCREMENTS AND ARE AN UPPER BOUND ON WHAT REAL
  HARDWARE DELIVERS. GRACE-FO IMU1B reports FiltAng, an angle passed through
  a selectable second-order low-pass filter, and a low-pass attenuates
  exactly the high-frequency content the coning term exists to capture. The
  published error terms of multi-sample coning algorithms assume unfiltered
  increments and DO NOT TRANSFER to that input; this toolbox has not measured
  the gain on filtered data and quotes no figure for it.

  Sign continuity and unit norm are properties of composeRotationVectors and
  are stated there.

ERRORS

referenceSystem:integrateAttitudeIncrements:wrongQuaternionSize
  referenceSystem:integrateAttitudeIncrements:wrongIncrementSize
  referenceSystem:integrateAttitudeIncrements:tooFewEpochs
  referenceSystem:integrateAttitudeIncrements:sizeMismatch
  referenceSystem:integrateAttitudeIncrements:samplesNotIncreasing

REFERENCES

Bortz, J. E. (1971), A new mathematical formulation for strapdown inertial
  navigation, IEEE Trans. Aerospace Electron. Syst. AES-7(1), 61-66.
  Savage, P. G. (1998), Strapdown inertial navigation integration algorithm
  design, part 1, J. Guidance Control Dyn. 21(1), 19-28.

EXAMPLE

fileName = fullfile("test", "fixtures", "imu1bGraceC.txt");
  imu = referenceSystem.readImu1b(fileName);
  [deltaTheta, epoch] = referenceSystem.graceFoImuIncrements(imu);
  q = referenceSystem.integrateAttitudeIncrements([1; 0; 0; 0], ...
      deltaTheta, epoch);

KEYWORDS

attitude, delta-theta, increment, coning, strapdown, gyro, quaternion

See also INTEGRATEATTITUDE, GRACEFOIMUINCREMENTS, ATTITUDECONVENTION.

referenceSystem.wanderAzimuthRotation

Wander-azimuth local-level mechanisation, free of the polar singularity.

SYNTAX

R = wanderAzimuthRotation(origin, wanderAngle) returns the 3x3xN rotation
  from the earth-fixed frame to the wander-azimuth local-level frame at each
  origin. The wander-azimuth frame exists for one reason: it is not slaved to
  north, so it has no singularity at the pole, where a north-slaved
  local-level frame must spin at a rate that diverges as tan(latitude).

  [R, rates] = wanderAzimuthRotation(origin, wanderAngle, 'Velocity', v) also
  returns the earth rate and the transport rate resolved in that frame, and
  the rate at which the wander angle itself must be propagated.

INPUT ARGUMENTS

Name          Size          Type     Units    Description
  ----          ----          ----     -----    -----------
  origin        Nx2 or Nx3    double   rad|deg  One origin per ROW: column 1
                                                geodetic latitude, column 2
                                                longitude, optional column 3
                                                ellipsoidal height in metres.
                                                Height defaults to zero and
                                                affects only the transport
                                                rate, never the rotation.
  wanderAngle   Nx1 or 1x1    double   rad|deg  Wander angle, measured from
                                                the local-level frame towards
                                                the wander frame about the
                                                vertical. A scalar applies to
                                                every origin.

OPTIONAL NAME-VALUE ARGUMENTS

Name         Size          Type            Default                Description
  ----         ----          ----            -------                -----------
  Velocity     Nx2 or Nx3    double          zeros                  Ground
                                                                    velocity in
                                                                    the LOCAL
                                                                    LEVEL frame
                                                                    named by the
                                                                    convention,
                                                                    m/s.
  Ellipsoid    1x1           string|struct   "GRS80"                Name or
                                                                    struct.
  AngleUnit    1x1           string          "rad"                  Unit of the
                                                                    input angles.
  Convention   1x1           struct          attitudeConvention()   .localFrame
                                                                    is read.

OUTPUT ARGUMENTS

Name              Size     Type     Units    Description
  ----              ----     ----     -----    -----------
  R                 3x3xN    double   -        Earth-fixed to wander-azimuth
                                               rotation, one page per origin.
  rates             1x1      struct   -        Angular rates in the WANDER
                                               frame.
  .earth            3xN      double   rad/s    omega_ie, the earth rate.
  .transport        3xN      double   rad/s    omega_ew, the transport rate.
                                               ITS VERTICAL COMPONENT IS
                                               EXACTLY ZERO, by definition of
                                               the mechanisation.
  .wanderAngleRate  1xN      double   rad/s    d(wanderAngle)/dt, which the
                                               caller must integrate.
  .total            3xN      double   rad/s    omega_iw = earth + transport,
                                               what a gyro senses when the
                                               platform is stationary in the
                                               wander frame.
  info              1x1      struct   -        Diagnostics.
  .localFrame       1x1      string   -        "ENU" or "NED".
  .northSlavedVertical 1xN   double   rad/s    The vertical transport rate a
                                               NORTH-SLAVED frame would need.
                                               This is the term that diverges
                                               at the pole and the number the
                                               mechanisation exists to avoid.
  .primeVerticalRadius 1xN   double   m        R_N + h.
  .meridianRadius   1xN      double   m        R_M + h.

METHOD

THE FRAME. The wander frame is the local-level frame rotated about the
  local vertical by the wander angle, so

      R = rotationZ(wanderAngle) * rotationEcefToEnu(origin)      (ENU)
      R = rotationZ(wanderAngle) * rotationEcefToNed(origin)      (NED)

  in the frame (passive) sense, and the elementary rotation comes from L4b
  rather than being written out. Under ENU the third axis is Up and a
  positive wander angle turns the first axis from East towards North; under
  NED the third axis is Down, so the same positive angle turns the first axis
  from North towards East. The vertical axis is unchanged either way, which
  is what makes the composition a rotation about it.

  THE EARTH RATE. The earth rotates about the polar axis, so in ENU
  omega_ie = omega_E [0; cos(lat); sin(lat)] and in NED
  omega_ie = omega_E [cos(lat); 0; -sin(lat)]. Both are then rotated into the
  wander frame by the same rotationZ. At the equator the vector lies wholly
  along North; at the pole wholly along the vertical; both are asserted in
  the test suite because they are the two cases a reader can check by hand.

  THE TRANSPORT RATE, AND WHY THIS FRAME EXISTS. A north-slaved local-level
  frame moving over the ellipsoid must rotate relative to the earth at

      omega_en^ENU = [-vN/(R_M + h); vE/(R_N + h); vE*tan(lat)/(R_N + h)]

  whose VERTICAL component carries tan(lat) and therefore diverges at the
  pole: at 89.999 degrees and 100 m/s east it is 0.895 rad/s, which is
  51 degrees per second of frame rotation for a frame that is meant to be
  locally stationary. The wander-azimuth mechanisation removes it by
  DEFINITION: the frame is declared not to rotate about the vertical relative
  to the earth, so the vertical component of omega_ew is exactly zero and the
  rotation that would have been applied to the frame is carried instead by
  the wander angle,

      d(wanderAngle)/dt = -vE*tan(lat)/(R_N + h)

  which the caller integrates alongside the attitude. Nothing is lost and
  nothing diverges: the singular quantity has been moved from a frame
  definition, where it is unavoidable, into a scalar the user may propagate
  or reset at will. The horizontal components are unchanged and are simply
  rotated into the wander frame.

  info.northSlavedVertical reports the term that was removed, so the benefit
  of the mechanisation is visible rather than asserted.

  R_N is the prime-vertical radius of curvature a/sqrt(1 - e^2 sin^2(lat))
  and R_M the meridian radius a(1 - e^2)/(1 - e^2 sin^2(lat))^(3/2), both
  from ellipsoidParameters, plus the ellipsoidal height.

ACCURACY

Exact to machine precision given the inputs: the rotation is one elementary
  rotation composed with a local-level rotation, and the rates are closed
  forms. Verified against an independent Python implementation at 52 N,
  13 E, 100 m with a 37 degree wander angle and a (200 E, 150 N) m/s
  velocity: the rotation agrees to 2.2e-16 elementwise with orthogonality
  residual 2.2e-16, and every rate component to 1e-21 rad/s absolute.

  THE EARTH RATE IS THE NOMINAL ONE AND THAT IS A CHOICE, NOT AN OVERSIGHT.
  iersConstants.earthRotationRate is the IERS Table 1.1 mean angular
  velocity, 7.292115e-5 rad/s. The true instantaneous rate differs by the
  length-of-day variation, up to about 2.2 ms/day in the supplied EOP series,
  which is 2.5e-8 relative - 1.8e-12 rad/s. Over an hour that is 6.6e-9 rad
  of unmodelled frame rotation, four orders below any inertial-grade gyro
  bias, so the nominal value is entirely adequate for inertial navigation. It
  is NOT adequate for geodetic rotation: a user needing the true rate must
  take it from the LOD-corrected ERA rate of rotationItrsToGcrs (P9, D20),
  where the same 2.5e-8 is 10 micrometres per second at the surface and the
  toolbox insists on modelling it.

  The rate expressions assume the velocity is the ground velocity relative to
  the earth. A velocity given relative to the air or to an inertial frame is
  a different quantity and will produce a transport rate that is wrong by the
  difference; nothing here can detect that.

ERRORS

referenceSystem:wanderAzimuthRotation:wrongOriginSize
  referenceSystem:wanderAzimuthRotation:wrongAngleSize
  referenceSystem:wanderAzimuthRotation:wrongVelocitySize
  referenceSystem:wanderAzimuthRotation:sizeMismatch
  referenceSystem:wanderAzimuthRotation:localFrameRequired

REFERENCES

D. H. Titterton and J. L. Weston (2004), Strapdown Inertial Navigation
  Technology, 2nd ed., Sect. 3.5, 5.5 and 11.4 on wander azimuth.
  P. D. Groves (2013), Principles of GNSS, Inertial and Multisensor
  Integrated Navigation Systems, 2nd ed., Sect. 2.4 and 5.4.
  IERS Conventions (2010), IERS Technical Note 36, Table 1.1, for omega_E.

EXAMPLE

[R, rates] = referenceSystem.wanderAzimuthRotation([52 13 100], 37, ...
      'AngleUnit', "deg", 'Velocity', [200 150]);

KEYWORDS

wander azimuth, free azimuth, local level, transport rate, earth rate,
  strapdown, inertial navigation, polar, ENU, NED, ECEF, ITRS

See also ROTATIONECEFTOENU, ROTATIONECEFTONED, ROTATIONNAVTOBODY,
  INTEGRATEATTITUDE, IERSCONSTANTS.

referenceSystem.readImu1b

Read a GRACE-FO IMU1B filtered-gyro-angle product.

SYNTAX

imu = readImu1b(fileName) reads the ASCII IMU1B product named by fileName
  and returns the filtered gyro angles with their epochs, exactly as the file
  states them: one record per gyro per sample, angles in degrees, epochs in
  GPS time. Nothing is differenced, unwrapped, resampled or projected here;
  graceFoImuIncrements does that.

  [imu, info] = readImu1b(fileName) also returns what the file was found to
  contain and what was checked.

INPUT ARGUMENTS

Name       Size   Type            Units  Description
  ----       ----   ----            -----  -----------
  fileName   1x1    string | char   -      Path to an IMU1B ASCII file.

OUTPUT ARGUMENTS

Name                Size   Type     Units  Description
  ----                ----   ----     -----  -----------
  imu                 1x1    struct   -      The product.
  .epoch              1x1    struct   -      Canonical epoch, N entries, GPS.
  .gyroId             Nx1    double   -      Gyro number, 1 to 4.
  .filteredAngle      Nx1    double   deg    The FiltAng field, VERBATIM.
                                             Accumulated, not an increment.
  .qualityFlag        Nx1    string   -      The eight-character qualflg.
  .satellite          1x1    string   -      "C" or "D", from column 4.
  .meta               1x1    struct   -      Provenance.
  .meta.file          1x1    string   -      Absolute path read.
  .meta.product       1x1    string   -      "IMU1B".
  .meta.timeReference 1x1    string   -      "G", the value of column 3.
  .meta.angleUnit     1x1    string   -      "degrees".
  .meta.declaredRecordCount 1x1 double -     num_records from the header.
  info                1x1    struct   -      Diagnostics.
  .recordCount        1x1    double   -      Records read.
  .gyroIdPresent      1xG    double   -      Sorted gyro numbers found.
  .recordsPerGyro     1xG    double   -      Count for each of them.
  .sampleIntervalMedian 1x1  double   s      Median spacing per gyro series.
  .sampleIntervalRange 1x2   double   s      Smallest and largest spacing.
  .largestGap         1x1    double   s      Largest spacing in any series.
  .flaggedRecordCount 1x1    double   -      Records whose qualflg is not
                                             all zeros.

METHOD

THE RECORD LAYOUT IS ASSERTED IN TWO INDEPENDENT WAYS AND TRUSTED IN
  NEITHER ALONE. Handbook section 3.4.2 warns that a reader should not be
  built to depend on the header, and a reader that ignores the header cannot
  notice a layout change at all. So the seven expected column names are held
  here, the header's variables block must agree with them in order when it is
  present, and the first data record must independently have seven fields of
  the right kinds. A file that fails either check is refused rather than
  indexed.

  THE TIME SCALE IS READ FROM COLUMN 3 AND NOT FROM THE HEADER, BECAUSE THE
  HEADER OF A REAL FILE IS WRONG ABOUT IT. IMU1B shares its format with
  IMU1A, and the shipped 2020-01-01 GRACE C product carries the inherited
  long_name "... in OBC Time" for column 1 while column 3 reads "G" and
  handbook section 4.2.10 states that IMU1B time tags are in GPS Time. A
  reader that believed the header would tag a GPS series as OBC time, which
  nothing downstream could detect. Column 3 must read "G": an IMU1A file
  needs TIM1B and CLK1B to reach GPS time and this toolbox does not do that
  conversion, so it is refused by name rather than read as if it were 1B.

  THE EPOCH IS BUILT THROUGH THE PACKAGE CONSTRUCTOR. GRACE-FO counts GPS
  seconds from 2000-01-01T12:00:00 GPS, which is exactly what
  j2000SecondsToEpoch means by its "GPS" scale, so the integer and fractional
  columns are recombined and handed over without any leap-second arithmetic
  here.

  THE DECLARED RECORD COUNT IS CHECKED. A truncated file parses perfectly and
  yields a shorter arc, which is a defect that looks like a short day.

ACCURACY

Lossless. The angle is the file's decimal converted to the nearest double;
  the shipped values carry fifteen significant digits, below the resolution
  of a double at 5757 deg (9.1e-13 deg), so the conversion is exact to the
  printed precision.

  The epoch is exact to the microsecond the file states: the fractional
  column is an integer number of microseconds, and it enters the seconds
  field of the split epoch, where one unit in the last place is 1.5e-11 s.

ERRORS

referenceSystem:readImu1b:fileNotFound
  referenceSystem:readImu1b:headerNotTerminated
  referenceSystem:readImu1b:unexpectedLayout
  referenceSystem:readImu1b:wrongTimeReference
  referenceSystem:readImu1b:mixedSatellite
  referenceSystem:readImu1b:recordCountMismatch
  referenceSystem:readImu1b:noData

REFERENCES

Wen, H. Y., et al. (2019), GRACE-FO Level-1 Data Product User Handbook,
  JPL D-56935, sections 3.4.2, 3.4.3 and 4.2.10.

EXAMPLE

fileName = fullfile("test", "fixtures", "imu1bGraceC.txt");
  [imu, info] = referenceSystem.readImu1b(fileName);
  disp(info.recordsPerGyro);

KEYWORDS

GRACE-FO, IMU1B, gyro, filtered angle, delta-theta, attitude

See also GRACEFOIMUINCREMENTS, GRACEFOIMUALIGNMENT, READEOP.

referenceSystem.graceFoImuAlignment

Gyro axes and IMU-to-Satellite-Frame rotation for GRACE-FO.

SYNTAX

alignment = graceFoImuAlignment(satellite) returns the measured directions
  of the four IMU gyro axes in the IMU frame, and the measured rotation from
  the IMU frame to the Satellite Frame, for the named GRACE-FO satellite.
  Both are ground-calibration constants; neither varies with time.

  alignment = graceFoImuAlignment("list") returns a table of the supported
  satellite identifiers instead.

INPUT ARGUMENTS

Name        Size   Type            Units  Description
  ----        ----   ----            -----  -----------
  satellite   1x1    string | char   -      "C" or "D", any case, or "list".
                                            This is the GRACEFO_id carried in
                                            column 4 of every Level-1
                                            product record.

OUTPUT ARGUMENTS

Name                    Size   Type     Units  Description
  ----                    ----   ----     -----  -----------
  alignment               1x1    struct   -      Alignment constants.
  .satellite              1x1    string   -      Canonical "C" or "D".
  .gyroAxis               3x4    double   -      Unit direction of each gyro
                                                 axis in the IMU frame, one
                                                 COLUMN per gyro, gyro 1 in
                                                 column 1.
  .imuToSatelliteFrame    3x3    double   -      Rotation carrying a vector
                                                 resolved in the IMU frame to
                                                 the Satellite Frame.
  .airbusSatelliteNumber  1x1    double   -      1 or 2, the row of the
                                                 handbook tables this came
                                                 from. See METHOD.
  .source                 1x1    string   -      Citation of the tables.

  For satellite == "list" the output is instead a 2x3 table with variables
  satellite, airbusSatelliteNumber and description.

METHOD

THE FOUR GYROS ARE A TETRAHEDRON, NOT AN ORTHOGONAL TRIAD. The four axes of
  .gyroAxis are mutually separated by the tetrahedral angle acos(-1/3) =
  109.4712 deg; measured from the shipped table the twelve pairwise angles lie
  between 109.4341 and 109.5045 deg. Any three of them span the space, which
  is what makes the fourth redundant rather than necessary. Decision D83
  governs which three are used and what the fourth is used for; see
  graceFoImuIncrements.

  .imuToSatelliteFrame is a proper rotation to the digits published: the
  worst element of R'*R - I is 7.2e-16 for satellite C and 6.3e-16 for
  satellite D, and both determinants are +1 to sixteen digits. It is applied
  as v_sf = R * v_imu.

  THE SATELLITE FRAME AND THE SCIENCE REFERENCE FRAME SHARE THEIR AXES.
  Handbook section 3.2.3 defines the SRF as having the same origin and the
  same coordinate axes as the SF, and states that all Level-1B products are
  provided in the SRF. No rotation therefore separates the output of
  graceFoImuIncrements from the frame the SCA1B attitude quaternions refer to.

  THE HANDBOOK TABLES ARE KEYED BY AIRBUS SATELLITE NUMBER AND THE DATA IS
  KEYED BY SCIENCE IDENTIFIER, AND THE BRIDGE IS TABLE 3. Tables 23 and 24
  name their rows "Satellite 1" and "Satellite 2", taken from the Airbus
  alignment report; the Level-1 products name their satellite "C" or "D".
  Handbook Table 3 maps the Astrium/Airbus system nomenclature FM 1 to GRACE C
  and FM 2 to GRACE D, so satellite 1 is C and satellite 2 is D.

  THAT MAPPING IS AN INFERENCE AND IT CANNOT BE CHECKED AGAINST A
  THREE-GYRO FILE, WHICH IS WHY IT IS STATED HERE RATHER THAN ASSUMED. Three
  gyros give three equations in three unknowns, so any alignment reproduces
  the measurements exactly and leaves no residual to look at. The two
  satellites' tables differ by a rotation of 214.5 arcsec, which is far too
  small to notice in a single epoch and large enough to matter: see the
  ACCURACY block of graceFoImuIncrements for what it costs over a day. A file
  carrying gyro 4 would discriminate them through the D83 residual.

ACCURACY

The tables are reproduced to every digit published. They are ground
  calibration values and the handbook states no uncertainty for them, so
  none is quoted here; an in-flight alignment estimate is outside this
  toolbox.

  The axis directions are used as published and are NOT re-normalised. Each
  column of the shipped table already has unit norm to 1e-9, and forcing the
  norm would silently change a published constant to hide a discrepancy that
  should be visible instead.

ERRORS

referenceSystem:graceFoImuAlignment:unknownSatellite

REFERENCES

Wen, H. Y., et al. (2019), GRACE-FO Level-1 Data Product User Handbook,
  JPL D-56935, Tables 23 and 24 (section 4.2.10) and Table 3 (section 2.5.1).
  Fischer, D. (2017), AOCS Sensor Alignment, GFO-TN-ADSF-AO-50002328 Issue 3,
  Airbus DS GmbH — the source of Tables 23 and 24.

EXAMPLE

alignment = referenceSystem.graceFoImuAlignment("C");
  axisAngles = acosd(alignment.gyroAxis(:,1)' * alignment.gyroAxis);

KEYWORDS

GRACE-FO, IMU, gyro, tetrahedron, alignment, satellite frame, SRF

See also GRACEFOIMUINCREMENTS, READIMU1B, INTEGRATEATTITUDE.

referenceSystem.graceFoImuIncrements

Satellite-Frame angle increments from GRACE-FO IMU1B.

SYNTAX

[deltaTheta, epoch] = graceFoImuIncrements(imu) turns the accumulated,
  per-gyro, staggered, degree-valued filtered angles of an IMU1B reading into
  integrated angle increments of the body about the Satellite Frame axes, in
  radians, over a single common epoch grid. Increment k spans epoch k to
  epoch k+1, so deltaTheta has one column fewer than epoch has entries.

  [deltaTheta, epoch, info] = graceFoImuIncrements(...) also returns the
  counter wraps that were repaired, the gyros used, and the gyro-4 residual
  where a fourth gyro was present.

INPUT ARGUMENTS

Name   Size   Type     Units  Description
  ----   ----   ----     -----  -----------
  imu    1x1    struct   -      Reading from readImu1b. Fields .epoch,
                                .gyroId, .filteredAngle and .satellite are
                                read; .qualityFlag and .meta are not.

OPTIONAL NAME-VALUE ARGUMENTS

Name             Size   Type     Default   Description
  ----             ----   ----     -------   -----------
  Method           1x1    string   "pchip"   Interpolant onto the common
                                             grid: "pchip", "spline" or
                                             "linear". See ACCURACY: at the
                                             native 1.1 ms stagger the three
                                             are indistinguishable.
  Epoch            1x1    struct   []        Common grid to resolve onto.
                                             Default is the epochs of the
                                             lowest-numbered gyro of the
                                             triad, clipped to the span all
                                             three cover.
  WrapThreshold    1x1    double   180       Increment magnitude, degrees,
                                             above which a sample is a
                                             counter wrap and not motion.
  MaxGap           1x1    double   1         Largest spacing, seconds, that
                                             may be interpolated across.
  Alignment        1x1    struct   []        Override for
                                             graceFoImuAlignment. Its
                                             .satellite must match the data.

OUTPUT ARGUMENTS

Name                    Size     Type     Units  Description
  ----                    ----     ----     -----  -----------
  deltaTheta              3x(N-1)  double   rad    Integral of the body
                                                   angular velocity over each
                                                   interval, resolved in the
                                                   Satellite Frame.
  epoch                   1x1      struct   -      Common grid, N entries,
                                                   GPS scale.
  info                    1x1      struct   -      Diagnostics.
  .satellite              1x1      string   -      "C" or "D".
  .gyroUsed               1x3      double   -      Always [1 2 3] (D83).
  .gyroPresent            1xG      double   -      What the file held.
  .alignmentSource        1x1      string   -      Citation of the tables.
  .interpolationMethod    1x1      string   -      Method that ran.
  .maxInterpolationShift  1x1      double   s      Largest distance any gyro
                                                   sample was moved.
  .largestGap             1x1      double   s      Largest grid spacing.
  .wrapCount              1x1      double   -      Counter wraps repaired.
  .wrap                   1xW      struct   -      One entry per wrap, with
                                                   fields gyroId, sampleIndex,
                                                   observedJump (deg),
                                                   substitutedIncrement (deg)
                                                   and impliedSpan (deg).
  .gyroFourResidual       1x1      struct   -      .available, and when true
                                                   .rms and .largest, rad.
  .triadConditionNumber   1x1      double   -      Noise amplification of the
                                                   three-axis solve.

METHOD

FIVE THINGS HAPPEN, IN THIS ORDER, AND THE ORDER IS THE DESIGN.

  (1) UNWRAP, PER GYRO, ON ITS OWN TIME TAGS. FiltAng is an accumulated
  counter, not an increment, and it rolls over. Handbook Table 23's field
  declares valid_range -5758,5758, and that is a declared bound and NOT the
  turning point: the shipped 2020-01-01 GRACE C product turns at +5757.074194
  and resumes at -5757.075213, a jump of -11514.149406 deg. Subtracting the
  declared 2*5758 = 11516 deg would leave 1.844 deg in one increment, which
  integrates to an attitude that is smooth, orthogonal and wrong. So the span
  is never assumed. A wrapped increment is detected on magnitude and REPLACED
  by the local continuation of the neighbouring increments, and the span this
  implies is reported in info.wrap rather than used.

  (2) BUILD ONE COMMON GRID. The three gyros are not sampled together: they
  stagger by about 1.1 ms and 2.3 ms, which is a systematic inter-axis offset
  and not noise, so it does not average away.

  (3) INTERPOLATE THE ANGLE, THEN DIFFERENCE — NOT DIFFERENCE, THEN
  INTERPOLATE THE RATE. The whole value of an integrated-angle input is that
  increments telescope: their sum over any span is the total angle turned,
  exactly. Interpolating a rate and multiplying by dt breaks that and
  reintroduces the sampling error this function exists to remove. A precision
  test asserts the telescoping to machine precision.

  (4) CONVERT DEGREES TO RADIANS. Handbook section 3.2.1 states that all
  Level-1 angles are radians and FiltAng is the exception, declaring
  units: degrees in its own variables block. It is the one field a converter
  is most likely to pass through untouched.

  (5) PROJECT, AND THE PROJECTION OF AN INCREMENT IS EXACT. Gyro j measures
  the component of the body rate along a FIXED axis a_j, so its accumulated
  increment is a_j' * integral(omega dt) with the axis outside the integral.
  Three such equations give integral(omega dt) in the IMU frame with no
  approximation whatever, and Table 24 carries it to the Satellite Frame.

  WHAT THIS FUNCTION RETURNS IS NOT A ROTATION VECTOR. It is the integral of
  the angular velocity, which equals the rotation vector only when the axis
  does not move within the interval. The difference is the coning term and it
  belongs to the attitude integrator, not here; see integrateAttitude, whose
  rotation-vector method applies exactly that correction.

  GYRO 4 IS A DIAGNOSTIC AND NEVER A CONTRIBUTOR (DECISION D83). The solution
  always uses the fixed triad 1, 2, 3. Where a fourth gyro is present its
  increment is compared against the triad's prediction and the residual is
  reported. A least-squares solution over whatever gyros are present at each
  epoch is the obvious design and it is wrong: each gyro carries its own
  bias, three-gyro and four-gyro solutions absorb those biases differently,
  and the estimated rate therefore STEPS when the fourth sensor appears and
  steps back when it leaves. Integrated, a train of steps is a random walk
  manufactured by the estimator out of continuous data, and the attitude
  stays smooth and orthogonal throughout.

  FEWER THAN THREE GYROS IS REFUSED. Two axes determine a plane; a third
  component assumed to complete it is a guess wearing the shape of a
  measurement.

ACCURACY

THE INTERPOLATION ONTO THE COMMON GRID IS NOT WHAT LIMITS THIS. Measured on
  the shipped GRACE C product by holding one sample out of each series and
  predicting it from its neighbours across a full 125 ms sample, the residual
  is 1.2e-05 to 1.7e-05 deg for "spline", "pchip" and "linear" alike — the
  three agree because the residual is the gyro's own noise and not the
  truncation of any of them. The distance actually interpolated is the
  stagger, at most 2.3 ms, where linear truncation is of order 4e-09 deg.
  The Method option therefore exists for a caller-supplied coarse grid and
  changes nothing at the native cadence. "pchip" is the default because it
  cannot overshoot, so a data gap cannot manufacture a plausible increment.

  THE REPAIRED WRAP CARRIES THE SCATTER OF THE NEIGHBOURING INCREMENTS. On
  the shipped product the increments of gyro 1 either side of the wrap are
  0.006469 deg with a standard deviation of 9.7e-05 deg, so the substituted
  increment is good to about 0.35 arcsec, against 11514 deg if the wrap were
  missed and 1.844 deg if the declared counter range were used as the span.
  There is one such interval per wrap; the shipped day has one, and the
  fixture that carries it reports an implied span of 11514.1559 deg.

  THE INTER-AXIS STAGGER, IF LEFT UNCORRECTED, IS 0.21 ARCSEC. At the
  observed 0.0518 deg/s a 1.13 ms offset is 5.85e-05 deg. It is a systematic
  offset between axes, so it biases rather than averages.

  FILTERING LIMITS WHAT ANY OF THIS CAN BUY, AND IT IS STATED HERE RATHER
  THAN BURIED. FiltAng is passed through a selectable second-order low-pass
  filter before it is reported. A low-pass on the angle attenuates exactly
  the high-frequency content that coning compensation exists to capture, so
  the published error terms of multi-sample coning algorithms — which assume
  unfiltered increments — DO NOT TRANSFER to this input and are not quoted.
  The increments here remain a better input than a sampled rate, because they
  are integrals rather than point values, but the size of that gain on
  filtered data has not been measured by this toolbox and no figure is given.

  THE ALIGNMENT TABLES DOMINATE EVERYTHING ABOVE. Solving the shipped GRACE C
  file with satellite D's tables changes the recovered rate by 1.16e-04 deg/s
  out of 0.0635, which is 0.18 per cent and invisible at any single epoch, and
  accumulates to 10.0 deg of attitude error per day. Transposing Table 24
  costs 328 arcsec for satellite C and 61 arcsec for satellite D, and is
  likewise invisible in the recovered rate magnitude because that matrix is
  nearly symmetric. Both are why the satellite is read from the data and the
  alignment is never a free argument.

  THE CHAIN IS CONFIRMED AGAINST PHYSICS THE TOOLBOX WAS NOT TOLD. The rate
  recovered from the shipped product is [+0.00010, +0.063457, +0.00018] deg/s
  — pitch about the Satellite Frame Y axis, which is the nadir-pointing
  attitude the handbook describes — and 0.063458 deg/s is a 94.55 min period,
  a 6875.0 km semi-major axis and a 496.8 km altitude for a satellite
  launched to 491.5 km nineteen months earlier.

  The three-axis solve amplifies gyro noise by at most cond(G) = 2.0004.

ERRORS

referenceSystem:graceFoImuIncrements:missingGyro
  referenceSystem:graceFoImuIncrements:missingField
  referenceSystem:graceFoImuIncrements:tooFewSamples
  referenceSystem:graceFoImuIncrements:unsortedEpoch
  referenceSystem:graceFoImuIncrements:gapTooLarge
  referenceSystem:graceFoImuIncrements:epochOutOfRange
  referenceSystem:graceFoImuIncrements:alignmentMismatch
  referenceSystem:graceFoImuIncrements:wrongScale

REFERENCES

Wen, H. Y., et al. (2019), GRACE-FO Level-1 Data Product User Handbook,
  JPL D-56935, sections 3.2.1, 3.3.3 and 4.2.10, Tables 23 and 24.
  Savage, P. G. (1998), Strapdown inertial navigation integration algorithm
  design, part 1, J. Guidance Control Dyn. 21(1), for the increment-versus-
  rate argument. Its coning error terms assume unfiltered increments.

EXAMPLE

fileName = fullfile("test", "fixtures", "imu1bGraceC.txt");
  imu = referenceSystem.readImu1b(fileName);
  [deltaTheta, epoch, info] = referenceSystem.graceFoImuIncrements(imu);
  fprintf("%d wrap(s) repaired\n", info.wrapCount);

KEYWORDS

GRACE-FO, IMU1B, gyro, delta-theta, increment, coning, strapdown, attitude

See also READIMU1B, GRACEFOIMUALIGNMENT, INTEGRATEATTITUDE.

L5e - Solar-system ephemeris and the barycentric frame

referenceSystem.ephemerisBodies

Bodies an ephemeris provider can supply, with their accuracy.

SYNTAX

bodies = ephemerisBodies() lists the bodies of the bundled SOFA provider.
  bodies = ephemerisBodies(Name, Value) selects a different provider.

  This is the single authority for the mapping from a body NAME to the index
  the MEX gateway uses, to the NAIF identifier a kernel uses, and to the
  accuracy the provider achieves for that body (design principle 2).
  barycentricBodyState and geocentricBodyState both resolve their body
  argument here rather than carrying a second copy of the list.

INPUT ARGUMENTS

(none)

OPTIONAL NAME-VALUE ARGUMENTS

Name         Size    Type      Default   Description
  ----         ----    ----      -------   -----------
  Source       1x1     string    "sofa"    Provider: "sofa" or "mice".
  Ephemeris    1x1     struct    struct([]) Furnished kernel set from
                                           readEphemeris. Required for
                                           Source "mice", ignored otherwise.

OUTPUT ARGUMENTS

Name              Size   Type     Units  Description
  ----              ----   ----     -----  -----------
  bodies            Nx6    table    -      One row per supported body.
  .Body             Nx1    string   -      Canonical name, the accepted argument.
  .NaifId           Nx1    double   -      NAIF integer identifier of the body.
  .Model            Nx1    string   -      What actually produces the state.
  .PositionError    Nx1    double   m      Barycentric position error, RMS.
  .VelocityError    Nx1    double   m/s    Barycentric velocity error, RMS.
  .BodyIndex        Nx1    double   -      Gateway index; do not use elsewhere.

METHOD

The SOFA provider composes three models, because SOFA supplies three things
  and every other body is arithmetic on them:

    Earth     iauEpv00 pvb, the only barycentric vector SOFA produces
    Sun       pvb - pvh, since the Sun is exactly where the Earth's
              barycentric and heliocentric vectors differ. No solar theory is
              involved and the two errors partly cancel
    Moon      pvb + iauMoon98
    planets   (pvb - pvh) + iauPlan94, which is heliocentric

  THE NAIF IDENTIFIERS ARE THE ONES DE440 ACTUALLY CONTAINS, which is why
  Mercury is 1 and not 199. For Mercury and Venus the planet and its system
  barycentre coincide. For the outer planets they do not, and the SOFA models
  do not resolve the difference: Jupiter's system barycentre lies up to about
  200 km from the planet centre against a model error of 71700 km, so the
  distinction is meaningless at this tier. It is NOT meaningless under the
  MICE provider, where the caller must ask for 5 or for 599 deliberately.

  PositionError and VelocityError are the model's own published RMS PLUS the
  4.6 km and 1.4 mm/s of the Earth's barycentric vector, added LINEARLY rather
  than in quadrature. The two are not independent samples of one distribution
  - every body here is referred to the same pvb - so a linear sum is the
  defensible bound and a quadrature sum would be a flattering one.

ACCURACY

The figures are those published in the SOFA source headers, not estimates:
  iauEpv00 against DE405 over 1900-2100, iauPlan94 against DE200 over
  1960-2025, iauMoon98 against ELP/MPP02 over 1950-2100. Outside those
  intervals the errors grow and the gateway raises a status the calling
  function turns into a warning; iauEpv00 doubles by 1800 and 2200.

  THE WORST-CASE FIGURES ARE LARGER THAN THESE AND ARE NOT TABULATED HERE:
  iauEpv00's barycentric maximum is 13.4 km against an RMS of 4.6, and
  iauMoon98's is 31.7 km against 6.1. Take the tabulated numbers as typical
  and multiply by three for a bound.

  NO PROVIDER HERE IS ADEQUATE FOR GRACE-CLASS PRECISE ORBIT DETERMINATION.
  The lunar third-body term is the binding constraint: at GRACE altitude the
  Moon's differential acceleration is 1.19e-6 m/s2 and a position error dr
  propagates as 3*(dr/r)*a, so

    provider            Moon position error   acceleration error   verdict
    ------------------  -------------------   ------------------   -------
    SOFA iauMoon98      ~10.7 km              1.0e-10 m/s2         inadequate
    JPL DE440, ~1 km    ~1 km                 9e-12 m/s2           adequate
    JPL DE440, ~1 m     ~1 m                  9e-15 m/s2           the target (D13)

  The accelerometer noise floor is about 1e-10 m/s2/sqrt(Hz). The SOFA
  provider sits ON that floor and is supplied so that the toolbox works out
  of the box and for coarse, simulation and geometry work. Use the MICE
  provider for anything that will be compared against real observations.

ERRORS

referenceSystem:ephemerisBodies:unknownSource
  referenceSystem:ephemerisBodies:ephemerisRequired

REFERENCES

IAU SOFA release 20231011, iauEpv00, iauPlan94 and iauMoon98 headers.
  Moisson, X. and Bretagnon, P., 2001, Celest. Mech. Dyn. Astron. 80, 205.
  Simon, J.L. et al., 1994, Astron. Astrophys. 282, 663.
  Meeus, J., Astronomical Algorithms, 2nd ed., Willmann-Bell, 1998, p. 337.
  IAU 2012 Resolution B2 (the astronomical unit as a defining constant).

EXAMPLE

bodies = referenceSystem.ephemerisBodies();
  disp(bodies(bodies.PositionError < 1e4, ["Body" "PositionError"]));

KEYWORDS

ephemeris, solar system, body, BCRS, barycentre, SOFA, MICE, NAIF, DE440

See also BARYCENTRICBODYSTATE, GEOCENTRICBODYSTATE, READEPHEMERIS.

referenceSystem.barycentricBodyState

Position and velocity of a solar-system body in the BCRS.

SYNTAX

position = barycentricBodyState(body, epoch)
  [position, velocity] = barycentricBodyState(body, epoch)
  [position, velocity, info] = barycentricBodyState(body, epoch, Name, Value)

  The state is referred to the solar-system barycentre and oriented with the
  BCRS, which is ICRS-aligned. Body names come from ephemerisBodies.

INPUT ARGUMENTS

Name     Size   Type            Units  Description
  ----     ----   ----            -----  -----------
  body     1x1    string | char   -      Body name, any case, e.g. "moon".
  epoch    1x1    struct          -      Epoch struct, N entries, any scale
                                         except UT1 (see ERRORS).

OPTIONAL NAME-VALUE ARGUMENTS

Name         Size   Type     Default     Description
  ----         ----   ----     -------     -----------
  Source       1x1    string   "sofa"      Provider: "sofa" or "mice".
  Ephemeris    1x1    struct   struct([])  Furnished kernels from
                                           readEphemeris; required for "mice".

OUTPUT ARGUMENTS

Name                Size   Type     Units  Description
  ----                ----   ----     -----  -----------
  position            3xN    double   m      BCRS position, one column per epoch.
  velocity            3xN    double   m/s    BCRS velocity, one column per epoch.
  info                1x1    struct   -      Fields listed below.
  .body               1x1    string   -      Canonical body name resolved.
  .naifId             1x1    double   -      NAIF identifier of that body.
  .source             1x1    string   -      Provider actually used.
  .model              1x1    string   -      What produced the numbers.
  .frame              1x1    string   -      "BCRS".
  .origin             1x1    string   -      "solar system barycentre".
  .epochScale         1x1    string   -      "TDB", the scale evaluated.
  .positionError      1x1    double   m      RMS bound, from ephemerisBodies.
  .velocityError      1x1    double   m/s    RMS bound, from ephemerisBodies.
  .frameBiasApplied   1x1    logical  -      False for "sofa"; see ACCURACY.

METHOD

The function owns the frame and time-scale semantics and delegates the
  arithmetic, which is decision D26: TDB argument, BCRS orientation, SI units,
  epoch handling and the accuracy statement are the toolbox's competence; the
  evaluation of a planetary theory or an SPK segment is not.

  The epoch is converted to TDB with convertTimeScale before evaluation, so a
  caller may pass TT, TAI, UTC, GPS or any other supported scale and get the
  same instant. TT differs from TDB by at most 1.7 ms, which is 51 m of Earth
  motion - three orders below the SOFA provider's error and one order ABOVE
  the MICE provider's, which is why the conversion is done rather than waved
  away.

  Outputs are 3xN with one column per epoch, matching the L2 convention that
  applyRotation and rotationItrsToGcrs already use, NOT the Nx3 convention of
  L3 and L4 (decision D65). A body state is consumed next to a GCRS satellite
  vector, so it takes the shape of one.

ACCURACY

See ephemerisBodies for the per-body table and for the reason no provider
  here reaches the GRACE-class tier. info.positionError carries the number for
  the body actually asked for, on every call, so it never has to be looked up.

  THE FRAME BIAS IS NOT APPLIED BY THE "sofa" PROVIDER AND info SAYS SO.
  iauEpv00 and iauMoon98 are BCRS and GCRS oriented, but iauPlan94 is referred
  to the mean equator and equinox of J2000.0, which differs from the ICRS by
  about 23 mas. That is 6.7 km at Mercury against a model error of 339 km, and
  500 km at Neptune against 158000 km - between one and three orders inside
  the model error for every planet. Applying the rotation would imply a
  precision the theory does not have while introducing a sign that nothing
  available here could check. The MICE provider returns ICRF directly and
  reports frameBiasApplied as true because the question does not arise.

  The velocity is per second of TDB. TDB is by construction a linear scaling
  of TCB chosen to track TT, so its second is an SI second at the geoid to
  within the periodic terms and no rate factor is applied in the conversion
  from au/day. A rate factor at the L_B level would be 4.6e-4 m/s on the
  Earth's velocity and is NOT what the au/day unit of these theories means.

ERRORS

referenceSystem:barycentricBodyState:unknownBody
  referenceSystem:barycentricBodyState:wrongScale
      Raised for a UT1 epoch. UT1 reaches TDB only through Delta UT1, which
      this function does not accept, and taking Delta UT1 as zero is a silent
      error of up to 0.9 s - 27 km of Earth motion, five times the provider's
      own error. Convert deliberately with convertTimeScale first.
  referenceSystem:barycentricBodyState:gatewayMissing
  referenceSystem:barycentricBodyState:providerNotAvailable
  referenceSystem:ephemerisBodies:unknownSource

WARNINGS

referenceSystem:barycentricBodyState:outsideModelRange
      Once per call, whatever N is: at least one epoch lies outside the
      interval over which the model's stated accuracy holds.
  referenceSystem:barycentricBodyState:seriesNotConverged

REFERENCES

IERS Conventions (2010), IERS Technical Note 36, Ch. 10.
  IAU 2000 Resolution B1.3 (the BCRS and GCRS).
  IAU SOFA release 20231011, iauEpv00, iauPlan94, iauMoon98.

EXAMPLE

t = referenceSystem.datetimeToEpoch(datetime(2023, 3, 1), "TT");
  [r, v, info] = referenceSystem.barycentricBodyState("moon", t);
  fprintf('%s to %.0f m RMS\n', info.model, info.positionError);

KEYWORDS

BCRS, barycentre, ephemeris, solar system, third body, TDB, SOFA, MICE

See also GEOCENTRICBODYSTATE, EPHEMERISBODIES, READEPHEMERIS, CONVERTTIMESCALE.

referenceSystem.geocentricBodyState

Position and velocity of a solar-system body from the Earth.

SYNTAX

position = geocentricBodyState(body, epoch)
  [position, velocity] = geocentricBodyState(body, epoch)
  [position, velocity, info] = geocentricBodyState(body, epoch, Name, Value)

  The state is the BCRS coordinate difference between the body and the Earth's
  centre of mass. READ THE ACCURACY BLOCK BEFORE CALLING IT A GCRS STATE: it
  is not one, and the difference is stated there with its size.

INPUT ARGUMENTS

Name     Size   Type            Units  Description
  ----     ----   ----            -----  -----------
  body     1x1    string | char   -      Body name, any case, e.g. "moon".
  epoch    1x1    struct          -      Epoch struct, N entries, any scale
                                         except UT1.

OPTIONAL NAME-VALUE ARGUMENTS

Name         Size   Type     Default     Description
  ----         ----   ----     -------     -----------
  Source       1x1    string   "sofa"      Provider: "sofa" or "mice".
  Ephemeris    1x1    struct   struct([])  Furnished kernels from
                                           readEphemeris; required for "mice".

OUTPUT ARGUMENTS

Name                Size   Type     Units  Description
  ----                ----   ----     -----  -----------
  position            3xN    double   m      Body minus Earth, one column per epoch.
  velocity            3xN    double   m/s    Its time derivative.
  info                1x1    struct   -      As barycentricBodyState, with
                                             .frame naming the caveat below.

METHOD

Both states are evaluated at the same TDB instant and differenced. For the
  Moon under the "sofa" provider this returns iauMoon98 exactly, because the
  Moon's barycentric vector was built as the Earth's plus iauMoon98 and the
  subtraction removes what the addition put there - a round trip that a
  precision test asserts to 1e-5 m, which is the roundoff of an addition and
  subtraction at the 1e11 m scale and not a tolerance chosen to pass.

ACCURACY

THIS IS A COORDINATE DIFFERENCE IN THE BCRS, NOT A GCRS STATE, AND THE TWO
  ARE NOT THE SAME VECTOR. The transformation to GCRS spatial coordinates
  carries a Lorentz term and a scale term of combined relative size about
  1.5e-8 (the constant L_C), so the GCRS distance exceeds this one by

    body      distance      omitted term
    -------   ----------    ------------
    Moon      3.8e8 m       about 6 m
    Sun       1.5e11 m      about 2.2 km
    Jupiter   7.8e11 m      about 12 km

  For the "sofa" provider every one of those is far below the model error
  tabulated by ephemerisBodies - 6 m against 10700 m for the Moon - so the
  distinction cannot be observed. UNDER THE "mice" PROVIDER IT IS THE
  DOMINANT ERROR, and 6 m on the Moon is well above the 1 m tier decision D13
  adopts as the design target.

  NO 'Frame' OPTION IS OFFERED AND THAT IS A DECISION, NOT AN OMISSION. The
  BCRS-to-GCRS transformation of IERS Conventions Ch. 10 is an expansion in
  the distance from the Earth and is valid only in its vicinity; applying it
  to Jupiter's geocentric vector would be using a formula outside its domain
  and getting a smooth, plausible, wrong answer - this domain's signature
  failure. A caller who needs rigorous GCRS coordinates for a near-Earth body
  applies bcrsToGcrs to this result and reads that function's own stated
  validity limit.

  Everything ephemerisBodies says about the provider tiers applies unchanged;
  info.positionError carries the number for the body asked for.

ERRORS

referenceSystem:geocentricBodyState:unknownBody
  referenceSystem:geocentricBodyState:wrongScale
  referenceSystem:geocentricBodyState:gatewayMissing
  referenceSystem:geocentricBodyState:providerNotAvailable

WARNINGS

referenceSystem:geocentricBodyState:outsideModelRange
  referenceSystem:geocentricBodyState:seriesNotConverged

REFERENCES

IERS Conventions (2010), IERS Technical Note 36, Ch. 10, Eq. (10.12).
  IAU 2000 Resolution B1.3 and B1.5 (L_C and the BCRS/GCRS relation).
  IAU SOFA release 20231011, iauEpv00, iauPlan94, iauMoon98.

EXAMPLE

t = referenceSystem.datetimeToEpoch(datetime(2023, 3, 1), "TT");
  rMoon = referenceSystem.geocentricBodyState("moon", t);
  fprintf('geocentric lunar distance %.1f km\n', vecnorm(rMoon)/1000);

KEYWORDS

geocentric, GCRS, BCRS, ephemeris, third body, Moon, Sun, TDB

See also BARYCENTRICBODYSTATE, EPHEMERISBODIES, READEPHEMERIS.

referenceSystem.readEphemeris

Furnish NAIF kernels and record what was furnished.

SYNTAX

ephemeris = readEphemeris(kernelFile)
  ephemeris = readEphemeris(kernelFile, Name, Value)

  The only cspice_furnsh wrapper the toolbox permits (decision D26). It loads
  the kernels, reads each one's comment area, records identity rather than
  content, and refuses combinations that would be wrong in ways nothing
  downstream could notice.

INPUT ARGUMENTS

Name         Size   Type              Units  Description
  ----         ----   ----              -----  -----------
  kernelFile   1xK    string | char     -      Kernel paths, in load order.

OPTIONAL NAME-VALUE ARGUMENTS

Name              Size   Type      Default  Description
  ----              ----   ----      -------  -----------
  ExpectedEphemeris 1x1    string    ""       Planetary ephemeris the kernels
                                              must name, e.g. "DE440". Empty
                                              takes the name from the first
                                              SPK and requires the rest to
                                              agree with it.
  AllowGenerations  1xG    string    []       Generations the caller has
                                              DECIDED belong together, e.g.
                                              ["DE440" "DE441"]. A mixed set
                                              is always accepted; naming it
                                              here stops the warning, for
                                              exactly that set and nothing
                                              wider. Empty warns on any
                                              mixture, which is the default.
  Digest            1x1    logical   true     Compute a SHA-256 of each file.

OUTPUT ARGUMENTS

Name              Size   Type      Units  Description
  ----              ----   ----      -----  -----------
  ephemeris         1x1    struct    -      Handle for the body-state functions.
  .meta             1x1    struct    -      Provenance, fields below.
  .meta.file        Kx1    string    -      Absolute paths as furnished.
  .meta.digest      Kx1    string    -      SHA-256, or "" when Digest false.
  .meta.ephemeris   1x1    string    -      Planetary ephemeris named by the
                                            SPK comment areas, or the value of
                                            ExpectedEphemeris when none names
                                            one. Read generationVerified
                                            before believing it.
  .meta.generations 1xG    string    -      Every distinct planetary ephemeris
                                            named across the comment areas.
                                            Normally one entry; more only
                                            when AllowGenerations lifted the
                                            D15 refusal.
  .meta.generationVerified
                    1x1    logical   -      True when the name was READ from a
                                            comment area; false when nothing
                                            could be read and the field is the
                                            caller's own declaration (D15).
  .meta.toolkit     1x1    string    -      cspice_tkvrsn('TOOLKIT').
  .meta.bodies      Nx6    table     -      Bodies the set covers, in the
                                            ephemerisBodies layout.

METHOD

IDENTITY TRAVELS AND BULK DOES NOT. de440.bsp is about 2.6 GB and
  sb441-n16.bsp about 650 MB against a whole toolbox of roughly two megabytes.
  Nothing here copies, caches or bundles a kernel; meta records the filename
  and a digest, which is what a result must carry to be reproducible and what
  an archive can afford to carry. This is the rule data/README.md states and
  the same rule mexSourceDigest applies to the C sources.

  THE KERNEL PAIRING IS MEASURED, NOT DECLARED (decision D15, and D110 for
  what happens when it disagrees: a warning, never a refusal), mechanism added
  rev. 1.127). SB441-N16 pairs with DE440/441 and SB431-N16 with DE430; mixing
  generations shifts the barycentre by of order 100 km, and nothing downstream
  would report it. Each SPK names the planetary ephemeris it expects in its
  own comment area, which cspice_dafec reads, so the pairing is checked
  against what the files say rather than against what the caller believes.

  A LEAPSECONDS KERNEL IS REFUSED (decision D89), AND THAT IS A SEAM DECISION
  RATHER THAN AN INCONVENIENCE. cspice_spkezr takes TDB seconds past J2000
  directly and convertTimeScale already reaches TDB, so a .tls would install a
  second leap-second authority beside leapSecondTable - finding M-2 in a new
  place, and a silent one, because the two agree until the day a
  leapSecondTable("load") override is installed and SPICE does not see it.
  Seam contract S1 item 2 exists to make that impossible by construction.

  THE TOOLKIT VERSION IS A CHECKED PRECONDITION AND NOT AN ASSUMPTION, because
  pck00011.tpc's Mars system data used with Toolkit N0066 or earlier may cause
  fatal run-time memory corruption in the calling process rather than a wrong
  number. NAIF ships pck00011_n0066.tpc for that case. The check costs one
  call to cspice_tkvrsn and this function refuses rather than proceeds.

ACCURACY

Not applicable; this function computes nothing. The accuracy of a state
  obtained through the furnished kernels is the kernel's own - about 1 m for
  DE440 in the current century, the tier decision D13 adopts as the target -
  and it is reported by ephemerisBodies for the resulting set, not here.

ERRORS

referenceSystem:readEphemeris:miceUnavailable
  referenceSystem:readEphemeris:fileNotFound
  referenceSystem:readEphemeris:leapSecondKernelRefused
  referenceSystem:readEphemeris:mismatchedGeneration
  referenceSystem:readEphemeris:toolkitTooOld

  WARNINGS
  referenceSystem:readEphemeris:mixedGenerations
      The furnished kernels name more than one planetary ephemeris. A
      WARNING and not an error since decision D110: the choice of models and
      of their combination belongs to the caller, and comparing two
      generations is ordinary work. Silenced for a set named in
      AllowGenerations.
  referenceSystem:readEphemeris:generationNotVerified
      ExpectedEphemeris was given and no comment area names a generation, so
      the pairing could not be measured. A warning and not an error: a kernel
      without that metadata is unusual, not wrong, and refusing it would make
      a good file unusable over a comment.

REFERENCES

NAIF SPICE Toolkit, MICE for MATLAB, https://naif.jpl.nasa.gov/naif/toolkit_MATLAB.html
  NAIF generic kernels, https://naif.jpl.nasa.gov/pub/naif/generic_kernels/
  JPL SSD small-body SPKs, https://ssd.jpl.nasa.gov/ftp/eph/small_bodies/asteroids_de441/
  Folkner, W.M. et al., 2014, IPN Progress Report 42-196 (DE430/DE431).
  Handover decisions D13, D15, D26, D89 and section H.7.1.

EXAMPLE

eph = referenceSystem.readEphemeris("data/de440s.bsp");
  r = referenceSystem.geocentricBodyState("moon", t, Source = "mice", ...
      Ephemeris = eph);

KEYWORDS

SPK, kernel, NAIF, MICE, DE440, ephemeris, provenance, digest, furnsh

See also BARYCENTRICBODYSTATE, GEOCENTRICBODYSTATE, EPHEMERISBODIES.

Developed by Matthias Weigelt with the assistance of Claude Opus 5 (Anthropic)
Created: 2026-07-28 11:10 (CEST, UTC+02:00)

referenceSystem.downloadKernels

Fetch the SPICE kernels the MICE provider needs, or list them.

SYNTAX

manifest = downloadKernels()
  manifest = downloadKernels(Set = "orientation", Mode = "download")
  manifest = downloadKernels(Mode = "verify")

  THE DEFAULT MODE IS "list" AND DOWNLOADS NOTHING. One entry in this
  catalogue is 650 MB, so the exploratory call has to be the harmless one;
  asking for bytes is deliberate, in Mode "download".

  This function is the SINGLE AUTHORITY for where these files come from. The
  table in data/README.md and section H.7.1 of the handover describe them;
  this catalogue is what a machine acts on, and a prose table that no code
  reads is exactly how H.7.1 came to carry a wrong row for four sessions
  (obligation O26).

INPUT ARGUMENTS

(none)

OPTIONAL NAME-VALUE ARGUMENTS

Name         Size   Type              Default    Description
  ----         ----   ----              -------    -----------
  Set          1x1    string            "minimal"  How much content:
                                                   "minimal", "orientation",
                                                   "smallBodies" or "all".
                                                   These ACCUMULATE.
  Span         1x1    string            "short"    Which issue of the
                                                   planetary ephemeris:
                                                   "short", "full" or
                                                   "both".
  Mode         1x1    string            "list"     "list", "verify" or
                                                   "download".
  CacheDir     1x1    string            data/      Destination folder.
  Timeout      1x1    double            900        Seconds per file.
  Overwrite    1x1    logical           false      Refetch files already there.
  Announce     1x1    function_handle   no-op      Called as
                                                   Announce(name, bytes, index,
                                                   total) before each transfer.

OUTPUT ARGUMENTS

Name                 Size   Type      Units  Description
  ----                 ----   ----      -----  -----------
  manifest             Nx9    table     -      One row per catalogue entry.
  .File                Nx1    string    -      Kernel file name.
  .Url                 Nx1    string    -      Where it comes from.
  .Set                 Nx1    string    -      Content group it belongs to.
  .Span                Nx1    string    -      "short", "full" or "any".
  .ApproximateBytes    Nx1    double    B      Size for planning. Measured
                                               where a real fetch has
                                               reported one; estimated
                                               otherwise, and estimates here
                                               have been wrong by 3x.
  .Purpose             Nx1    string    -      What needs it.
  .Note                Nx1    string    -      Traps and pairing rules.
  .Status              Nx1    string    -      "listed", "present", "fetched",
                                               "reachable", "FAILED | reason".
  .Bytes               Nx1    double    B      Actual size, once on disk.
  .Digest              Nx1    string    -      SHA-256, once on disk.

METHOD

TWO AXES, BECAUSE THERE ARE TWO INDEPENDENT QUESTIONS.

  Set says HOW MUCH CONTENT, and its values accumulate:

    "minimal"      the planetary ephemeris alone. All P22b needs.
    "orientation"  adds the text PCK, the binary lunar orientation and the
                   lunar frame kernel. What P23 needs.
    "all"          adds sb441-n16.bsp, about 650 MB, for work where the
                   sixteen D15 perturbing asteroids matter. "smallBodies"
                   is accepted as a synonym.

  "all" MEANS ALL (decision D99). Rev. 1.134 gave it the meaning "everything
  except the biggest file", wrote that into the help and added a test
  asserting it - documenting and enforcing a name that lied. The size was a
  real concern and that was a bad answer to it; the protection is that Mode
  defaults to "list", which shows the bill before anything moves.

  Span says WHICH ISSUE of the planetary ephemeris:

    "short"        de440s.bsp, 1849-12-26 to 2150-01-22, about 31 MB.
                   Ample for anything GRACE-era and the default.
    "full"         de440.bsp, 1550 to 2650, of order 120 MB. For epochs
                   outside the short file.
    "both"         both files. Decision D114.

  THE TWO FILES ARE THE SAME INTEGRATION - de440s.bsp is a time-restricted
  issue of de440.bsp - so in the overlap they answer identically and SPICE
  resolves an overlapping body from whichever was furnished last. Until
  rev. 1.191 that was taken as a reason to REFUSE both, and it is not one.
  D110 already settled the principle one level up, for whole generations:
  this toolbox prevents an UNKNOWING mixture, not a deliberate choice.
  Holding the small file for routine work and the long one for the
  occasional deep-time epoch is ordinary, and readEphemeris reports the
  generation of everything furnished either way. "both" is therefore
  offered and is not the default; "short" remains the default because it is
  what almost every caller needs.

  THE SIZE THAT ARGUED FOR THE REFUSAL WAS WRONG BY MORE THAN AN ORDER OF
  MAGNITUDE. This catalogue carried 2.6 GB for de440.bsp - which is the
  scale of DE441, not DE440 - and the help, the error message and decision
  D99's reasoning all leaned on it. de440s.bsp measured 31.2 MB for its
  300-year span; the same integration over DE440's 1100 years (Park et al.
  2021) scales to roughly 120 MB, which is what the catalogue now carries,
  MARKED AS DERIVED AND NOT MEASURED. Mode "verify" or a real fetch records
  the true figure, and until one does this number is an estimate like the
  others.

  sum(manifest.ApproximateBytes) is the planning figure for any combination.

  MODE "verify" ISSUES A HEAD REQUEST PER ENTRY AND DOWNLOADS NOTHING. It
  exists because the URLs below were transcribed from section H.7.1 and could
  not be checked when they were written - the machine that wrote them had no
  route to either host. THE FIRST CALLER WITH A NETWORK CLOSES O26 IN ONE
  CALL rather than discovering a bad path part-way through a 650 MB transfer.

  EVERY ENTRY IS ATTEMPTED AND EVERY FAILURE IS RECORDED BEFORE ANYTHING IS
  RAISED. A failure on the third of five files must not discard the two that
  succeeded - that is precisely what run 44 got wrong with the HORIZONS
  fetcher (rev. 1.130), and the manifest carries "FAILED | reason" rows for
  the same reason runTestSuite writes its report before reporting failure.

  NO LEAPSECONDS KERNEL APPEARS IN THIS CATALOGUE AND NONE MAY BE ADDED.
  Decision D89: a .tls would install a second leap-second authority beside
  leapSecondTable, silently, because the two agree until a
  leapSecondTable("load") override is installed and SPICE does not see it.
  readEphemeris refuses to furnish one, and a static test asserts that no
  .tls reaches this catalogue - a rule enforced at both ends of the path.

  Identity is recorded and bulk is not: each fetched file gets a SHA-256 in
  the manifest, which is what a result should carry (data/README.md).

ACCURACY

Not applicable; this function transfers files and computes no quantity.
  ApproximateBytes is the published size and is for planning only; Bytes is
  measured from the file on disk.

ERRORS

referenceSystem:downloadKernels:unknownSet
  referenceSystem:downloadKernels:unknownSpan
  referenceSystem:downloadKernels:unknownMode
  referenceSystem:downloadKernels:cacheNotWritable

WARNINGS

referenceSystem:downloadKernels:incompleteFetch
      At least one entry failed. THIS IS A WARNING AND NOT AN ERROR because
      the manifest is the artifact: a function that throws returns nothing,
      so raising here would destroy the record of everything that succeeded.
      Decision D98, taken after the first real run reported zero kernels
      while four were on disk. Inspect Status and retry the FAILED rows.

REFERENCES

NAIF generic kernels, https://naif.jpl.nasa.gov/pub/naif/generic_kernels/
  JPL SSD small-body ephemerides, https://ssd.jpl.nasa.gov/ftp/eph/
  Folkner, W. M. et al., 2014, IPN Progress Report 42-196 (DE430/DE431).
  Park, R. S. et al., 2021, Astron. J. 161, 105 (DE440 and DE441).
  Handover sections H.7.1, H.7.5; decisions D13, D15, D26, D89; O26.

EXAMPLE

referenceSystem.downloadKernels()                       % see the plan
  referenceSystem.downloadKernels(Mode = "verify")        % check the URLs
  referenceSystem.downloadKernels(Set = "orientation", Mode = "download")
  referenceSystem.downloadKernels(Set = "all", Span = "full")   % the lot

KEYWORDS

SPICE, kernel, NAIF, DE440, SPK, PCK, download, catalogue, provenance

See also READEPHEMERIS, DOWNLOADEOP, SETUPREFERENCESYSTEM, EPHEMERISBODIES.
