referenceSystem.gcrsToBcrs

Express a geocentric vector in barycentric (BCRS) coordinates.

SYNTAX

rBcrs = gcrsToBcrs(rGcrs, epoch, Scale = "tdbCompatible")
  [rBcrs, info] = gcrsToBcrs(rGcrs, epoch, Name, Value)

  Applies IERS Conventions (2010) Eq. (11.19), or Eq. (11.18) with
  Scale "none": the Lorentz contraction along the Earth's barycentric
  velocity, the scaling by the external gravitational potential, and - for
  "tdbCompatible" - the TT-to-TDB length scale L_C.

  IT RETURNS THE SAME SEPARATION VECTOR IN DIFFERENT COORDINATES, NOT A
  BARYCENTRIC POSITION. See METHOD; the distinction is four orders of
  magnitude wide and is the first thing to get right about this function.

INPUT ARGUMENTS

Name    Size   Type      Units  Description
  ----    ----   ----      -----  -----------
  rGcrs   3xN    double    m      Geocentric vector in the GCRS, one column
                                  per epoch, or one column for all epochs.
  vGcrs   3xN    double    m/s    Velocity per unit of the SOURCE frame's
                                  coordinate time. PASS [] FOR POSITION ONLY.
  epoch   1x1    struct    -      Epoch struct, N entries, any scale but UT1.

OPTIONAL NAME-VALUE ARGUMENTS

Name         Size   Type     Default     Description
  ----         ----   ----     -------     -----------
  Scale        1x1    string   NONE        Required. "tdbCompatible" applies
                                           Eq. (11.19); "none" applies
                                           Eq. (11.18). See ERRORS.
  Source       1x1    string   "sofa"      Ephemeris provider for U and V.
  Ephemeris    1x1    struct   struct([])  Kernels, for Source "mice".

OUTPUT ARGUMENTS

Name                   Size   Type     Units    Description
  ----                   ----   ----     -----    -----------
  rBcrs                  3xN    double   m        The vector in BCRS coordinates.
  info                   1x1    struct   -        Fields listed below.
  .scale                 1x1    string   -        The Scale actually applied.
  .scaleTerm             1x1    double   1        L_C, or 0 for "none".
  .externalPotential     1xN    double   m^2/s^2  U at the geocentre.
  .potentialBodies       1x1    string   -        Bodies summed into U.
  .reference             1x1    string   -        Equations implemented.
  .statedUncertainty     1x1    double   m        1e-3, the Conventions' own.

METHOD

THIS IS A CHANGE OF COORDINATES FOR A VECTOR, NOT A CHANGE OF ORIGIN. The
  barycentric POSITION of the point is

      xBcrs = barycentricBodyState("earth", epoch) + gcrsToBcrs(rGcrs, ...)

  and that addition is deliberately left to the caller, because the two
  quantities have accuracies four orders apart and combining them silently
  would hide which one dominates. This function is good to the Conventions'
  stated 1 mm; the Earth's barycentric position from the bundled SOFA
  provider is good to 4.6 km. Adding them without noticing produces a number
  whose 1 mm claim is nonsense.

  THE TRANSFORMATION IS EXACTLY LINEAR AND THAT IS WHY THE INVERSE IS EXACT.
  Both equations have the form r_out = M r_in with M = alpha*I + beta*V*V',
  a rank-one update of a scaled identity, so bcrsToGcrs inverts it in closed
  form by Sherman-Morrison rather than by negating parameters (decision D68's
  rule, met here by an exact solution rather than by an accurate one).

  U and V come from barycentricBodyState and geocentricBodyState, so P22a is
  a genuine dependency and not a convenience. They enter only at order 1/c^2,
  which is why the bundled provider suffices - see ACCURACY.

ACCURACY

The Conventions state their own uncertainty for Eq. (11.19) as BELOW 1 mm,
  and that is the accuracy of this function. Every quantity neglected inside
  it is smaller than that and is quantified in the private helper: the
  planets contribute at most 12 micrometres through U, the Moon 0.9
  micrometres, and the measured uncertainties of GM_sun and L_C are nine and
  six orders below the millimetre respectively.

  THE EPHEMERIS IS ALMOST IRRELEVANT HERE, WHICH IS COUNTER-INTUITIVE AND
  WORTH STATING. U and V reach the answer only through 1/c^2 terms, so their
  errors are suppressed by 1e-8. Measured against the bundled SOFA provider's
  own budget: 2.8 mm/s on the Earth's velocity moves the result by 4.8e-9 m,
  and 8.3 km on the solar distance by 3.5e-9 m - six orders inside the 1 mm.
  Do not reach for MICE on account of this function.

  TERM SIZES AT A MID-LATITUDE STATION (|r| = 6391 km), measured, so that the
  choice of Scale is a decision taken with the numbers in view:

    L_C scale term          -0.0947 m   (present only for "tdbCompatible")
    U/c^2 potential term    -0.0631 m
    Lorentz term             +0.0322 m  (maximum, when r is along V)
    total, "tdbCompatible"  -0.1715 m
    total, "none"           -0.0769 m

  THE VELOCITY IS NOW PROVIDED, FROM IAU 2000 RESOLUTION B1.3, AND THE TERM
  THAT COULD NOT BE PINNED DOWN AT REV. 1.132 IS ITS dT/dt FACTOR:
  1 - (v_E^2/2 + w_ext + a_E.r + v_E.v)/c^2, which is 1.5e-8 - on 31 km/s
  that is 4.7e-4 m/s, four thousand times the 1e-7 m/s a KBR reduction works
  at. Refusing to guess it was right. Validated by central difference at six
  step sizes: the residual falls by exactly four at each halving, order 2.00
  throughout, so it is the difference's own truncation and not a formula
  error. Superseded note follows.

  NO VELOCITY TRANSFORMATION IS PROVIDED AND THAT IS NOT AN OVERSIGHT. The
  IERS Conventions (2010) give Eqs. (11.18) and (11.19) for POSITION and
  state no transformation for velocity anywhere in Chapters 10 or 11.
  Differentiating (11.18) would need the Earth's barycentric acceleration and
  dU/dt, which are available, but also the rate relation between the two
  frames' time arguments, which is a convention question rather than an
  arithmetic one and whose size - d(TDB-TT)/dt is about 3.3e-10, i.e. 2.5e-6
  m/s at 7.5 km/s - is twenty-five times the 1e-7 m/s tier a KBR or LRI
  reduction works at. Supplying a velocity derived from a rule this document
  does not state would produce something smooth, self-consistent and wrong at
  exactly the level the function exists to reach.

ERRORS

referenceSystem:gcrsToBcrs:scaleRequired
      Scale has no default. The two settings differ by 9.5 cm at the Earth's
      surface, ninety-five times the formula's own uncertainty, and neither
      is visible in the result - decision D67's rule, applied again.
  referenceSystem:gcrsToBcrs:unknownScale
  referenceSystem:gcrsToBcrs:sizeMismatch
  referenceSystem:barycentricBodyState:wrongScale

REFERENCES

IERS Conventions (2010), IERS Technical Note 36, G. Petit and B. Luzum
      (eds.), Eq. (11.18) and Eq. (11.19), Section 11.2; Table 1.1.
  IAU 2000 Resolutions B1.3 and B1.5 (the BCRS, GCRS and L_C).
  Klioner, S. A. et al., 2010, Proc. IAU Symp. 261.

EXAMPLE

t = referenceSystem.datetimeToEpoch(datetime(2023, 3, 1), "TT");
  station = [4517590; 837897; 4442892];
  rBcrs = referenceSystem.gcrsToBcrs(station, t, Scale = "tdbCompatible");
  fprintf('length changes by %.4f m\n', norm(rBcrs) - norm(station));

KEYWORDS

BCRS, GCRS, barycentric, Lorentz, L_C, TT-compatible, TDB-compatible, scale

See also BCRSTOGCRS, BARYCENTRICBODYSTATE, GEOCENTRICBODYSTATE, ITERSCONSTANTS.

referenceSystem.bcrsToGcrs

Express a barycentric-coordinate vector in the GCRS.

SYNTAX

rGcrs = bcrsToGcrs(rBcrs, epoch, Scale = "tdbCompatible")
  [rGcrs, info] = bcrsToGcrs(rBcrs, epoch, Name, Value)

  The exact inverse of gcrsToBcrs: IERS Conventions (2010) Eq. (11.19), or
  Eq. (11.18) with Scale "none", solved rather than approximated.

  THE INPUT IS A SEPARATION VECTOR FROM THE GEOCENTRE EXPRESSED IN BCRS
  COORDINATES, NOT A BARYCENTRIC POSITION. Subtract the Earth's barycentric
  position first; see METHOD in gcrsToBcrs, where the distinction is four
  orders of magnitude wide.

INPUT ARGUMENTS

Name    Size   Type      Units  Description
  ----    ----   ----      -----  -----------
  rBcrs   3xN    double    m      Geocentric vector in BCRS coordinates, one
                                  column per epoch, or one column for all.
  vBcrs   3xN    double    m/s    Velocity per unit of the SOURCE frame's
                                  coordinate time. PASS [] FOR POSITION ONLY.
  epoch   1x1    struct    -      Epoch struct, N entries, any scale but UT1.

OPTIONAL NAME-VALUE ARGUMENTS

Name         Size   Type     Default     Description
  ----         ----   ----     -------     -----------
  Scale        1x1    string   NONE        Required; as gcrsToBcrs.
  Source       1x1    string   "sofa"      Ephemeris provider for U and V.
  Ephemeris    1x1    struct   struct([])  Kernels, for Source "mice".

OUTPUT ARGUMENTS

Name    Size   Type     Units  Description
  ----    ----   ----     -----  -----------
  rGcrs   3xN    double   m      The vector in GCRS coordinates.
  info    1x1    struct   -      As gcrsToBcrs.

METHOD

THE INVERSE IS EXACT, NOT ACCURATE, AND THAT IS THE POINT OF THIS FILE.
  Both Conventions equations are linear in the geocentric vector,
  r_out = M r_in with M = alpha*I + beta*V*V', a rank-one update of a scaled
  identity. Sherman-Morrison gives

      M^-1 = (1/alpha) [ I - beta V V' / (alpha + beta |V|^2) ]

  in closed form. Cross-checked in numpy against a dense inverse: both leave
  ||M M^-1 - I|| at 1.11e-16, so nothing is bought by forming the matrix.

  Decision D68 requires every inverse in this toolbox to be SOLVED and never
  obtained by negating the forward parameters. Negation would in fact be
  accurate here - the correction is 1.5e-8, so its square is 2e-16 relative,
  a nanometre at the Earth's surface - but an exact solution that costs the
  same removes the argument entirely, and a rule that is followed only where
  it is cheap is not a rule.

ACCURACY

The Conventions' own stated uncertainty, below 1 mm, exactly as gcrsToBcrs.
  The round trip through both functions closes to 9.3e-10 m at a
  mid-latitude station, which is roundoff at the 6.4e6 m scale and not a
  property of the formula.

  THE VELOCITY IS PROVIDED; see gcrsToBcrs for its source and validation.

ERRORS

referenceSystem:bcrsToGcrs:scaleRequired
  referenceSystem:bcrsToGcrs:unknownScale
  referenceSystem:bcrsToGcrs:sizeMismatch

REFERENCES

IERS Conventions (2010), IERS Technical Note 36, Eq. (11.18) and (11.19),
      Section 11.2; Table 1.1.
  Sherman, J. and Morrison, W. J., 1950, Ann. Math. Statist. 21(1), 124.

EXAMPLE

t = referenceSystem.datetimeToEpoch(datetime(2023, 3, 1), "TT");
  station = [4517590; 837897; 4442892];
  b = referenceSystem.gcrsToBcrs(station, t, Scale = "tdbCompatible");
  back = referenceSystem.bcrsToGcrs(b, t, Scale = "tdbCompatible");
  fprintf('round trip %.3e m\n', norm(back - station));

KEYWORDS

BCRS, GCRS, barycentric, Lorentz, L_C, inverse, Sherman-Morrison

See also GCRSTOBCRS, BARYCENTRICBODYSTATE, GEOCENTRICBODYSTATE, IERSCONSTANTS.

L5f - Body-fixed frames (IAU WGCCRE)

referenceSystem.bodyRotationElements

IAU WGCCRE rotational elements of the Sun and planets.

SYNTAX

[rightAscension, declination, primeMeridian] = bodyRotationElements(body, epoch)
  [..., info] = bodyRotationElements(body, epoch, Name, Value)

  Evaluates alpha0, delta0 and W of the IAU Working Group on Cartographic
  Coordinates and Rotational Elements, 2015 report, Table 1. These are the
  pole direction in the ICRF and the angle from the node Q to the prime
  meridian, measured along the body equator.

  TWO PROVIDERS, AND WHICH ONE IS RIGHT DEPENDS ON THE BODY.

    Source "iau"  (default)  WGCCRE Table 1, transcribed and bundled: the Sun
                             and the eight planets. Needs nothing installed.
    Source "pck"             Any body a furnished SPICE text PCK covers,
                             which is Tables 1, 2 and 3. Needs MICE and
                             pck00011.tpc.

  TABLES 2 AND 3 ARE DELIBERATELY NOT TRANSCRIBED (decision D100). They hold
  more than six hundred tabulated numbers against Table 1's ninety, and -
  this is the deciding fact - THERE IS NO INDEPENDENT ORACLE FOR THEM. Table
  1 was validated against orbital poles computed from barycentricBodyState,
  but de440s.bsp contains no satellite and no asteroid ephemerides, so for a
  satellite the only available check is cspice_pxform against pck00011.tpc:
  the very file a transcription would be copying. Shipping six hundred
  numbers whose only check is their own source is precisely what this project
  refuses, so those tables are READ FROM THE KERNEL instead. This is the same
  split barycentricBodyState already makes between "sofa" and "mice".

INPUT ARGUMENTS

Name    Size   Type            Units  Description
  ----    ----   ----            -----  -----------
  body    1x1    string | char   -      Body name, any case, e.g. "mars".
  epoch   1x1    struct          -      Epoch struct, N entries, any scale
                                        except UT1.

OPTIONAL NAME-VALUE ARGUMENTS

Name         Size   Type     Default     Description
  ----         ----   ----     -------     -----------
  Source       1x1    string   "iau"       "iau" or "pck".
  Ephemeris    1x1    struct   struct([])  Furnished kernels from
                                           readEphemeris; required for "pck".
  Frame        1x1    string   ""          Explicit SPICE frame name for
                                           Source "pck", overriding
                                           IAU_<BODY>. This is how the Moon
                                           and the Earth are reached; see
                                           ERRORS.

OUTPUT ARGUMENTS

Name              Size   Type     Units  Description
  ----              ----   ----     -----  -----------
  rightAscension    1xN    double   rad    alpha0, pole right ascension.
  declination       1xN    double   rad    delta0, pole declination.
  primeMeridian     1xN    double   rad    W, wrapped to [0, 2*pi).
  info              1x1    struct   -      Fields listed below.
  .body             1x1    string   -      Canonical name resolved.
  .rotationRate     1x1    double   rad/d  Wdot, the linear term. Sign gives
                                           the sense of rotation.
  .sense            1x1    string   -      "prograde" or "retrograde".
  .siderealPeriod   1x1    double   d      360/|Wdot|.
  .poleDefinition   1x1    string   -      Which pole this is. See ACCURACY.
  .source           1x1    string   -      Provider actually used.
  .frame            1x1    string   -      SPICE frame, or "" for "iau".
  .reference        1x1    string   -      The table implemented.
  .epochScale       1x1    string   -      "TDB".

METHOD

RADIANS OUT, DEGREES IN THE TABLE. The report tabulates degrees; the
  conversion happens once, here, so that no caller mixes units and no second
  copy of the table exists in other units. W is wrapped to [0, 2*pi) because
  it accumulates thousands of turns - Jupiter passes 10^6 degrees within
  three years of J2000 - and an unwrapped angle loses resolution to the
  floating-point exponent long before it loses meaning.

  Arguments are formed from the interval since J2000.0 TDB in days (d) and in
  Julian centuries of 36525 days (T), and WHICH OF THE TWO A PERIODIC TERM
  USES IS PART OF THE TABLE, not a convention: Mercury's M1..M5 advance in
  DAYS while Mars's, Jupiter's and Neptune's arguments advance in CENTURIES.
  Getting that wrong changes a term's period by a factor of 36525 and is the
  single most likely transcription error in this table, so each term carries
  its own unit rather than inheriting one.

ACCURACY

THE "iau" SOURCE COVERS WGCCRE TABLE 1 ONLY - THE SUN AND THE EIGHT
  PLANETS - AND THE "pck" SOURCE COVERS FAR MORE (open item B6, decision
  D100). Tables 2 and 3, which hold the satellites, dwarf planets and minor
  planets, are deliberately not transcribed: section 6 of the report uses the
  OPPOSITE pole convention for dwarf planets, minor planets and comets, so
  folding them into one table would put two conventions behind one argument
  value. The consequence a caller must know is that the two sources have
  DIFFERENT DOMAINS, not merely different accuracies: Phobos, Io, Titan,
  Ceres and Vesta resolve under Source = "pck" and are refused under
  Source = "iau". Until now only the error message said so; it is stated
  here as well, because a domain limit discovered from an error is a limit
  discovered late.

  The report states its expressions are generally accurate to ONE TENTH OF A
  DEGREE, with Mercury, Mars, Saturn and Uranus carrying more digits to
  reflect greater confidence. Two decimal digits are given for consistency
  and trailing zeros on Wdot are for computational consistency, NOT an
  indication of accuracy far from J2000. At Uranus the report notes the
  present uncertainty of the prime meridian exceeds a complete rotation.

  THE POLE IS THE ONE NORTH OF THE INVARIABLE PLANE, WHICH IS NOT THE
  RIGHT-HAND-RULE SPIN POLE FOR A RETROGRADE ROTATOR. For Venus and Uranus
  the two differ by 180 degrees, so the angle between this pole and the
  orbital pole is 2.6 and 82.2 degrees while the published axial tilts are
  177.4 and 97.8 - the supplements. info.poleDefinition states this on every
  call and info.sense gives the sense of rotation from the sign of Wdot.
  Section 6 of the report uses the OPPOSITE convention for dwarf planets,
  minor planets and comets, which is one reason Table 3 is not folded in here.

  THE TRANSCRIPTION WAS VALIDATED AGAINST QUANTITIES THE TABLE DOES NOT
  CONTAIN, because checking a transcription against a second copy of itself
  proves nothing. Each pole was compared with the ORBITAL pole computed as
  r x v from barycentricBodyState, and each rate with the published sidereal
  period:

    body      pole vs orbital pole      360/Wdot vs published period
    -------   --------------------      ----------------------------
    Mercury   0.034 deg vs 0.03          58.646146 d vs 58.646     2.5e-6
    Venus     2.638 vs 180-177.36       -243.018 d vs -243.025     2.7e-5
    Mars      25.192 vs 25.19             1.025957 d exact         3.9e-9
    Jupiter   3.119 vs 3.13               0.413538 d (System III)  4.1e-6
    Saturn    26.729 vs 26.73             0.444009 d (System III)  1.7e-6
    Uranus    82.230 vs 180-97.77        -0.718333 d              4.6e-6
    Neptune   28.318 vs 28.32             15.96630 h (System II)   8.8e-11

  The Neptune check first failed by 0.9 per cent against 16.11 h. That is the
  SYSTEM III period; the 2015 report adopted System II from Karkoschka (2011)
  and says so in section 5. The comparator was wrong, not the table - which
  is the kind of thing only a check against an outside quantity can tell you.

ERRORS

referenceSystem:bodyRotationElements:unknownBody
  referenceSystem:bodyRotationElements:earthNotTabulated
      The 2015 report REMOVED the approximate expressions for the Earth
      (section 10, item 2) because their accuracy was poor and they failed
      near J2000, yet were being used as a recommended model. Use
      rotationItrsToGcrs, which is what the report points to.
  referenceSystem:bodyRotationElements:moonNotTabulated
      The report also removed the low-precision lunar series (section 10,
      item 3): it is accurate to only about 150 m and the mean-Earth/polar
      axis frame must come from a lunar ephemeris. This is decision D14's
      non-defaulting lunar frame arriving from the authority rather than from
      a scoping choice.
  referenceSystem:bodyRotationElements:wrongScale

REFERENCES

Archinal, B. A. et al., 2018, Celest. Mech. Dyn. Astron. 130, 22:
      "Report of the IAU Working Group on Cartographic Coordinates and
      Rotational Elements: 2015", Table 1 and Sections 2, 5 and 10.
  Kuchynka, P. et al., 2014, Icarus 229, 340 (the Mars model).
  Karkoschka, E., 2011, Icarus 215, 439 (Neptune System II).
  Stark, A. et al., 2017, J. Geodesy (Mercury after MESSENGER).

EXAMPLE

t = referenceSystem.datetimeToEpoch(datetime(2023, 3, 1), "TT");
  [a, d, w, info] = referenceSystem.bodyRotationElements("mars", t);
  fprintf('%s, %s, sidereal day %.6f d\n', info.body, info.sense, ...
      info.siderealPeriod);

KEYWORDS

WGCCRE, IAU, rotational elements, pole, prime meridian, planet, ICRF, body-fixed

See also ROTATIONICRFTOBODYFIXED, BARYCENTRICBODYSTATE, ROTATIONITRSTOGCRS.

referenceSystem.rotationIcrfToBodyFixed

Rotation from the ICRF to a body-fixed frame.

SYNTAX

rotation = rotationIcrfToBodyFixed(body, epoch)
  [rotation, info] = rotationIcrfToBodyFixed(body, epoch, Name, Value)

  Builds the IAU WGCCRE body-fixed frame from the rotational elements of the
  2015 report, Table 1: z along the pole, x through the prime meridian, y
  completing a right-handed set.

      rotation = Rz(W) * Rx(pi/2 - delta0) * Rz(pi/2 + alpha0)

  The inverse is the transpose, as for every rotation in this toolbox.

INPUT ARGUMENTS

Name    Size   Type            Units  Description
  ----    ----   ----            -----  -----------
  body    1x1    string | char   -      Body name, any case, e.g. "mars".
  epoch   1x1    struct          -      Epoch struct, N entries, any scale
                                        except UT1.

OPTIONAL NAME-VALUE ARGUMENTS

Name         Size   Type     Default     Description
  ----         ----   ----     -------     -----------
  Source       1x1    string   "iau"       "iau" for the bundled WGCCRE
                                           Table 1, "pck" for any body a
                                           furnished SPICE PCK covers -
                                           Tables 1, 2 and 3.
  Ephemeris    1x1    struct   struct([])  Furnished kernels; needed for "pck".
  Frame        1x1    string   ""          Explicit SPICE frame, overriding
                                           IAU_<BODY>. Required for the Moon:
                                           "MOON_ME" or "MOON_PA".

OUTPUT ARGUMENTS

Name       Size     Type     Units  Description
  ----       ----     ----     -----  -----------
  rotation   3x3xN    double   -      One orthonormal matrix per epoch,
                                      mapping an ICRF column vector to the
                                      body-fixed frame.
  info       1x1      struct   -      As bodyRotationElements, plus
                                      .composition naming the product.

METHOD

THE TWO PROVIDERS TRAVEL IN OPPOSITE DIRECTIONS, AND THAT IS DELIBERATE.
  With Source "iau" the three angles are primary and this rotation is
  assembled from them; with Source "pck" the ROTATION is primary, because
  that is what cspice_pxform returns, and bodyRotationElements derives the
  angles from it instead. Each provider produces what its source actually
  holds, and a kernel-backed test compares the two for the Table 1 bodies
  where both are available - a check neither provider could perform alone.

  For Source "iau" the three angles come from bodyRotationElements, which is
  the single place the table is encoded. The geometry is the report's own Figure 1: the node Q
  of the body equator on the ICRF equator lies at alpha0 + 90 degrees, the
  inclination of the body equator to the celestial equator is 90 - delta0,
  and W is measured from Q to the prime meridian along the body equator.

  THE ROTATIONS ARE BUILT HERE AND NOT TAKEN FROM rotationX AND rotationZ,
  WHICH IS DELIBERATE AND NOT DUPLICATION. Those functions take their sense
  from attitudeConvention(), which a caller may set. The WGCCRE frame is
  defined by the report and cannot depend on a session preference: a body
  frame that silently transposed because somebody changed a global would be
  a wrong answer with no symptom. The matrices used here are frame
  (passive) rotations, fixed by this file, and the pole test below is what
  proves the composition rather than the comment.

  THE COMPOSITION IS ASSERTED BY A PROPERTY, NOT BY INSPECTION. Applying the
  result to the pole direction (alpha0, delta0) expressed in the ICRF must
  give exactly [0; 0; 1]. Any error in the order of the three rotations, in
  the 90-degree offsets, or in the sense of any one of them moves the pole
  off the z axis, so a single test covers all three.

ACCURACY

Entirely that of the elements: see bodyRotationElements, where the report's
  one-tenth-of-a-degree statement, the per-body validation against orbital
  poles, and the Uranus prime-meridian caveat are given in full. This
  function adds only orthonormal arithmetic, which is exact to roundoff -
  measured departure from orthonormality below 1e-15 and determinant within
  1e-15 of +1.

  THE FRAME IS THE CARTOGRAPHIC ONE, NOT A DYNAMICAL ONE. The report notes
  (section 2) that a principal-axis frame is not necessarily coincident with
  a frame defined by synchronous or resonant rotation, and that principal
  axis frames usually rely on a specific gravity field. For dynamical work,
  and for the Moon at any precision, use a frame from the ephemeris instead.

ERRORS

referenceSystem:bodyRotationElements:unknownBody
  referenceSystem:bodyRotationElements:earthNotTabulated
  referenceSystem:bodyRotationElements:moonNotTabulated
  referenceSystem:bodyRotationElements:wrongScale
      All raised by bodyRotationElements, which resolves the body. The
      identifiers name that function because that is where the refusal is
      decided; section C.2 binds an identifier to the deciding function.

REFERENCES

Archinal, B. A. et al., 2018, Celest. Mech. Dyn. Astron. 130, 22,
      Section 2 and Figure 1.

EXAMPLE

t = referenceSystem.datetimeToEpoch(datetime(2023, 3, 1), "TT");
  R = referenceSystem.rotationIcrfToBodyFixed("mars", t);
  rBodyFixed = R*rIcrf;          % and rIcrf = R.'*rBodyFixed

KEYWORDS

WGCCRE, IAU, body-fixed, planetocentric, rotation, ICRF, pole, prime meridian

See also BODYROTATIONELEMENTS, ROTATIONITRSTOGCRS, APPLYROTATION.

L6 - Dispatch and infrastructure

referenceSystem.convertCoordinates

Single point of access across the L3 and L4 transformations.

SYNTAX

y = convertCoordinates(x, fromFrame, toFrame) routes the Nx3 coordinates
  through the shortest chain of L3 and L4 functions that carries them from
  one frame or representation to the other.

  y = convertCoordinates(x, "ITRF2014", "ITRF2020", 'Epoch', 2020.0) changes
  the terrestrial realization; an epoch is REQUIRED for such a path and
  REFUSED for one that has no notion of time.

  adjacency = convertCoordinates('graph') returns the whole graph, each edge
  carrying the time semantics it declares, for documentation and for testing.

  [y, info] = convertCoordinates(...) also returns the path that was chosen,
  never printing it: this is library code (section B.1 principle 10).

INPUT ARGUMENTS

Name        Size   Type     Units      Description
  ----        ----   ----     -----      -----------
  x           Nx3    double   see below  Coordinates, one point per ROW, in
                                         whatever fromFrame means. Nx2 for the
                                         UTM and (with 'graph') no argument at
                                         all.
  fromFrame   1x1    string   -          Source frame or representation, or
                                         the literal "graph" as the only
                                         argument.
  toFrame     1x1    string   -          Target frame or representation.

OPTIONAL NAME-VALUE ARGUMENTS

Name           Size          Type            Default      Description
  ----           ----          ----            -------      -----------
  Epoch          1x1 or Nx1    double          []           Decimal year.
                                                            Required by, and
                                                            only by, a path
                                                            containing a
                                                            realization edge.
  Origin         1x3 or Nx3    double          []           Local frame origin
                                                            [lat lon h],
                                                            required by an ENU
                                                            or NED endpoint.
  Zone           1x1 or Nx1    double          []           UTM zone, required
                                                            leaving UTM.
  Hemisphere     1x1 or Nx1    double          []           +1 or -1, required
                                                            leaving UTM.
  Ellipsoid      1x1           string|struct   "GRS80"      Name or struct.
  AngleUnit      1x1           string          "rad"        Unit of every
                                                            angular quantity
                                                            in and out.
  Velocity       Nx3           double          zeros(0,3)   Velocities carried
                                                            through a
                                                            realization edge.
  Verbose        1x1           logical         false        Add the per-edge
                                                            record to info.

OUTPUT ARGUMENTS

Name             Size   Type     Units      Description
  ----             ----   ----     -----      -----------
  y                Nx3    double   see below  Coordinates in toFrame; Nx2 for
                                              a UTM target.
  info             1x1    struct   -          What the router did.
  .path            1xM    string   -          Canonical node names traversed.
  .timeKind        1xM-1  string   -          Per-edge time semantics.
  .edgeFunction    1xM-1  string   -          The function each leg called.
  .zone            Nx1    double   -          UTM zone, when the target is UTM.
  .hemisphere      Nx1    double   -          +1 north, -1 south, likewise.
  .latitudeBand    Nx1    string   -          MGRS band, likewise.
  .velocity        Nx3    double   m/a        Velocity, when one was carried.
  .edge            1xM-1  struct   -          Full edge records, 'Verbose' only.

METHOD

THIS FUNCTION CONTAINS NO TRANSFORMATION MATHEMATICS. It holds an
  adjacency, finds a shortest path by breadth-first search, checks that the
  supplied arguments match what the WHOLE path needs, and only then calls the
  L3 and L4 functions in turn. A layering test checks the claim mechanically
  rather than by review (seam item 1), and the edge functions are called by
  explicit qualified name rather than through str2func precisely so that the
  layering scan can see the dependencies: both source scanners blank string
  literals, so a dynamically dispatched toolbox would look dependency-free to
  the one test that guards this rule.

  EVERY EDGE DECLARES ITS TIME SEMANTICS (decision D29). The graph mixes two
  kinds of edge: pure geometry, which has no notion of time at all, and the
  similarity transforms between terrestrial realizations, which are
  parameterised by a decimal year. A router that walked both without
  distinguishing them would eventually apply an epoch to a leg that has none,
  or omit it from a leg that needs one, and both legs would "succeed". The
  timeKind of every edge on the chosen path is therefore collected BEFORE any
  of them executes, and a mismatch is an error. Nothing is coerced, no
  missing epoch is defaulted, no supplied one is ignored.

  THE GRAPH IS TWO DELIBERATELY DISCONNECTED COMPONENTS, and this is the
  least obvious decision in the file. The geometry nodes hang off the generic
  earth-fixed Cartesian node ITRS; the realization nodes connect only to each
  other. They are not joined, because joining them creates the path
  ITRF2014 -> GEODETIC -> ITRF2020, which costs no epoch, raises no error and
  silently reinterprets a coordinate as belonging to a realization it does
  not - D29's failure mode arriving through the back door, past the very
  check written to stop it. Shortest-path search would usually prefer the
  direct realization edge, but "usually" is not a guarantee, and a guarantee
  is available for free by not building the bridge. Requesting a path across
  the two therefore raises :noPath, and the message names the two-call
  recipe, which is what the user wanted anyway:

      x2 = convertCoordinates(x1, "ITRF2014", "ITRF2020", 'Epoch', 2020.0);
      g  = convertCoordinates(x2, "ITRS", "GEODETIC");

  ITRS <-> GCRS IS NOT AN EDGE (decision D29). It is the one transformation
  needing an epoch struct rather than a decimal year, an EOP table read from
  disk - an L1 file dependency inside an L6 dispatcher, the only place the
  call-downward rule is at risk - and it produces a rate the caller almost
  always needs and this interface cannot return. Asking for GCRS or ECI
  raises :useRotationFunction naming rotationItrsToGcrs and applyRotation.

  RSW IS NOT AN EDGE EITHER, and this departs from the P15 prompt's node
  list. An orbit frame is defined by a satellite STATE rather than by a frame
  parameter, and its only edge would run from GCRS, which is excluded above -
  so as a node it would be permanently unreachable and every request for it
  would return :noPath, which says nothing useful. It raises
  :useRotationFunction naming rotationGcrsToRsw instead. A good error beats a
  dead node.

  UTM IS LOSSY AND SAYS SO. The projection carries no height, so
  utmToGeodetic returns two columns and a path leaving UTM cannot continue to
  a three-column representation; the leg function raises its own
  wrongPointSize, which names itself and is clearer than anything this
  function could add. Leaving UTM also needs the zone and the hemisphere,
  which cannot be recovered from a northing because the false northing makes
  the hemispheres overlap.

ACCURACY

The router introduces no error of its own: it neither computes nor rounds
  anything, and each leg is executed exactly once with the caller's data. The
  accuracy of a result is the composition of the ACCURACY blocks of the
  functions named in info.edgeFunction, and a path of M legs accumulates M of
  them.

  The one thing worth stating quantitatively is that a chain is not free: the
  round trip ITRS -> GEODETIC -> ITRS closes to 1.4e-09 m, which is
  cartesianToGeodetic's own closure and not an addition by this function, and
  a path through UTM closes only to the 7.4e-04 m of the Krueger series.
  Shortest-path routing therefore also minimises accumulated error, which is
  a consequence of the unweighted search rather than a design goal, and would
  stop being true if any edge were ever weighted by anything but hop count.

ERRORS

referenceSystem:convertCoordinates:unknownFrame
  referenceSystem:convertCoordinates:noPath
  referenceSystem:convertCoordinates:missingOption
  referenceSystem:convertCoordinates:timeKindMismatch
  referenceSystem:convertCoordinates:useRotationFunction
  referenceSystem:convertCoordinates:unroutableEdge

REFERENCES

IERS Conventions (2010), IERS Technical Note 36, Ch. 4 for the terrestrial
  frames and Ch. 5 for the transformation excluded here.
  Z. Altamimi et al. (2023), ITRF2020, Journal of Geodesy 97(47).

EXAMPLE

g  = referenceSystem.convertCoordinates(x, "ITRS", "GEODETIC");
  x2 = referenceSystem.convertCoordinates(x, "ITRF2014", "ITRF2020", ...
      'Epoch', 2020.0);
  adjacency = referenceSystem.convertCoordinates('graph');

KEYWORDS

dispatch, frame, ECEF, ITRS, ECI, GCRS, geodetic, ENU, NED, UTM, spherical,
  ITRF, realization, conversion, graph

See also CARTESIANTOGEODETIC, CONVERTITRF, ROTATIONITRSTOGCRS, APPLYROTATION.

referenceSystem.mexInfo

State of the compiled MEX gateway and of the leap-second authority.

SYNTAX

mexState = mexInfo() reports whether the package-private MEX gateway is
  built, what it was built from, and how it is configured. It is the only
  public face of +referenceSystem/private/mexGateway, which is not callable
  from outside the package.

INPUT ARGUMENTS

(none)

OUTPUT ARGUMENTS

Name                       Size   Type      Units  Description
  ----                       ----   ----      -----  -----------
  mexState                   1x1    struct    -      Fields listed below.
  .built                     1x1    logical   -      True if the gateway exists.
  .file                      1x1    string    -      Expected gateway path.
  .buildDate                 1x1    string    -      Build date, ISO 8601, or ''.
  .sofaRelease               1x1    string    -      SOFA release, or ''.
  .openMp                    1x1    logical   -      True if built with OpenMP.
  .threadCount               1x1    double    -      Active OpenMP threads, NaN
                                                     if unknown.
  .compiler                1x1    string   -      Name of the SELECTED C
                                                  compiler, empty if none can
                                                  be interrogated. A variable
                                                  on Windows - MinGW and MSVC
                                                  differ in the math library
                                                  behind sin and cos and in
                                                  the OpenMP runtime - so a
                                                  timing comparison that does
                                                  not record it is comparing
                                                  two unknowns.
  .clampedToSingleThread     1x1    logical   -      True if D31 clamping is in
                                                     force inside a parfor worker.
  .leapSecondsValidThrough   1x1    string    -      Last date the table covers,
                                                     or '' if unknown.
  .leapSecondsSource         1x1    string    -      Authority of that table.
  .summary                   1x1    string    -      One line for the report header.
  .leapSecondsSummary        1x1    string    -      One line for the report header.

METHOD

The gateway is a build product, so its absence is a normal state, not an
  error: this function reports it and returns. Every field has the same class
  and size whether or not the gateway exists, so a caller never has to test
  for existence before indexing, and the test report can be written before any
  C code has been compiled.

  The fields that only the gateway itself can answer for - the SOFA release
  it was compiled against, the OpenMP thread count and whether the decision
  D31 clamp is in force - are filled by a mexGateway('info') query. A gateway
  that is present but does not load is reported as such rather than as
  absent, because the two have different repairs.

  The leap-second fields are not read from the gateway. They are asked of
  leapSecondTable, which owns them, so that this header and that function can
  never disagree; if leapSecondTable cannot answer, the state says so instead of
  guessing. A stated but unverified SOFA release or leap-second coverage in a
  test report is exactly the provenance defect this toolbox exists to
  prevent.

ACCURACY

Not applicable; the function reports build state and computes no numerical
  quantity. threadCount is NaN, and the two date fields are empty strings,
  whenever the value is genuinely unknown; this is the documented use of an
  undefined-but-valid value permitted by section C.6 rule 5.

ERRORS

(none) The function reports rather than raises: a missing gateway is a
  state, and every caller of this function needs the state, not an exception.

REFERENCES

Handover section C.4 (MEX rules), C.9 and decision D31 (thread clamping),
  section D.3 (the report header this function feeds).

EXAMPLE

state = referenceSystem.mexInfo();
  if ~state.built
      % referenceSystem.buildMex has not been run yet
  end

KEYWORDS

MEX, gateway, build, SOFA, OpenMP, threads, leap seconds, provenance

See also RUNTESTSUITE, TOOLBOXVERSION.

referenceSystem.mexSourceDigest

Content digest of every C source the gateway is built from.

SYNTAX

digest = mexSourceDigest(toolboxRoot) returns a number that changes when
  any byte of any file under mex/ changes, and when a file is added or
  removed. It is what setupReferenceSystem uses to decide whether the
  compiled gateway is stale.

  WHY NOT TIMESTAMPS
  ------------------
  Because they do not survive the way this toolbox is delivered. A ZIP entry
  stores naive local time with no time zone, so an archive built on a UTC
  machine and unpacked on a UTC+2 one produces sources that appear two hours
  OLDER than they are. Revisions 1.93, 1.94 and 1.99 each changed a C source,
  each shipped it, and none of them rebuilt: mex/rsApi.c arrived stamped
  15:44 against a gateway built at 17:26, so max(datenum) was behind the
  binary and the check reported current. Every time, the symptom was read as
  an installation problem and once it was written into the log as an
  obligation about stale caches. It was arithmetic on wall clocks.

  A content digest has no such failure mode. It is immune to the time zone,
  to clock skew between machines, to a git checkout that writes every file
  with the checkout time, and to an archive unpacked in any order.

INPUT ARGUMENTS

Name          Size   Type     Units  Description
  ----          ----   ----     -----  -----------
  toolboxRoot   1x1    string   -      Distribution folder holding mex/.

OUTPUT ARGUMENTS

Name     Size   Type     Units  Description
  ----     ----   ----     -----  -----------
  digest   1x1    double   -      Non-negative integer below 2^31. Equal
                                  digests mean the sources are unchanged;
                                  different digests mean they are not.

METHOD

Every .c and .h under mex/, at the top level and in every subfolder, sorted
  by path so the result cannot depend on the order the file system lists
  them. Each file contributes a position-weighted sum of its bytes together
  with its length and its name, and the file digests are folded together in
  sorted order.

  THE TOP LEVEL IS GLOBBED SEPARATELY FROM THE RECURSIVE PATTERN. Whether
  MATLAB's ** spans zero folder levels is not something to rely on from
  memory, and the answer decides whether mexGateway.c, rsCore.c, rsCheb.c and
  rsApi.c are seen at all - they are the only files here that ever change,
  since sofa/ and iers/ are vendored and frozen. The union is correct under
  either reading.

ACCURACY

Not a cryptographic hash and not intended as one. It answers "did these
  files change", not "did an adversary change them". A weighted sum detects
  any single-byte edit; two simultaneous edits that exactly cancel are
  possible in principle and are not a failure mode a build check needs to
  defend against.

ERRORS

referenceSystem:mexSourceDigest:noSources

REFERENCES

Handover revision 1.100 and the failures recorded at 1.85, 1.95 and 1.99.

EXAMPLE

digest = referenceSystem.mexSourceDigest(pwd);

KEYWORDS

build, staleness, digest, checksum, MEX

See also buildMex, setupReferenceSystem, mexInfo

referenceSystem.buildMex

Compile the package-private MEX gateway against the bundled SOFA source.

SYNTAX

buildReport = buildMex()
  buildReport = buildMex(Name, Value)

  Compiles every .c file at the top of mex/ together with every .c file in
  mex/sofa/ into one MEX file in +referenceSystem/private/, where it is
  callable from inside the package and from nowhere else. Run it once after
  unpacking the toolbox, and again whenever the C sources change.

INPUT ARGUMENTS

(none) The sources, the output folder and the SOFA release are all fixed by
  the layout of the distribution; nothing about the build is a caller's
  choice except the two switches below.

OPTIONAL NAME-VALUE ARGUMENTS

Name       Size   Type      Default  Description
  ----       ----   ----      -------  -----------
  OpenMp     1x1    logical   true     Attempt an OpenMP build. On failure
                                       the serial build is used instead and
                                       the fallback is reported.
  Debug      1x1    logical   false    Build with -g and without -DNDEBUG.

OUTPUT ARGUMENTS

Name                Size   Type      Units  Description
  ----                ----   ----      -----  -----------
  buildReport         1x1    struct    -      Fields below.
  .file               1x1    string    -      The MEX file written.
  .openMp             1x1    logical   -      Whether the BUILT BINARY has
                                               OpenMP, read back from the
                                               gateway, not inferred from the
                                               build command succeeding.
  .openMpFlags        1x1    string    -      Which flag spelling the
                                               platform actually honoured,
                                               or "" where OpenMP is absent.
  .openMpFallback     1x1    string    -      Why OpenMP is absent, or "".
  .sourceCount        1x1    double    -      Translation units compiled.
  .sofaRelease        1x1    string    -      Release reported by the gateway.
  .leapSecondsSummary 1x1    string    -      Coverage of the leap-second
                                              table the new build exposes.
  .duration           1x1    double    s      Wall-clock build time.

METHOD

The build is a plain mex call with three deliberate properties.

  The output goes to +referenceSystem/private/. A private folder is not on
  the MATLAB path and is visible only to the functions of its parent, so the
  gateway cannot be called by a user or by a test, and everything a caller
  legitimately needs from it is re-exported by mexInfo (handover section C.4).

  The misconfiguration of section C.5 rule 1 is rejected before anything is
  compiled. With +referenceSystem itself on the path every function becomes
  visible both qualified and unqualified, which reintroduces every shadowing
  problem the namespace was adopted to remove; building on top of that would
  produce a working binary in a broken installation.

  OpenMP is attempted and its absence is not an error. Apple clang ships
  without it, so on macOS the serial build is used directly rather than after
  a failed attempt, and the reason is reported rather than printed. Nothing
  in this function prints; section B.1 principle 10 has one exception in this
  toolbox and it is setupReferenceSystem, not this function.

  The result is verified by asking mexInfo, which queries the gateway itself,
  so a build that produced a file the process cannot load is a failure here
  rather than a puzzle later.

ACCURACY

Not applicable; the function compiles code and computes no numerical
  quantity. The numerical accuracy of what it produces is that of the
  unmodified SOFA release named in .sofaRelease.

ERRORS

referenceSystem:buildMex:packageFolderOnPath
  referenceSystem:buildMex:missingSource
  referenceSystem:buildMex:noCompiler
  referenceSystem:buildMex:outputFileLocked
      The gateway from an earlier build is still mapped into this process and
      the linker cannot overwrite it. Raised BEFORE mex is invoked where the
      preflight can see it, and also from either build attempt, so that a
      locked output is never reported as an OpenMP flag failure.
  referenceSystem:buildMex:buildFailed
  referenceSystem:buildMex:gatewayNotLoadable

REFERENCES

Handover sections C.4 (MEX rules), C.5 rule 1, C.9 and C.10.
  IAU SOFA release 20231011, bundled unmodified in mex/sofa/.

EXAMPLE

report = referenceSystem.buildMex();
  assert(report.openMp || strlength(report.openMpFallback) > 0);

KEYWORDS

MEX, build, compile, SOFA, OpenMP, gateway, installation

See also MEXINFO, LEAPSECONDS.

referenceSystem.buildDocumentation

Generate the function reference and the help search database.

SYNTAX

report = buildDocumentation() regenerates the function reference page from
  the help text of every package function, then rebuilds the Help browser
  search database, doing each only when its inputs are newer than its output.

  report = buildDocumentation('Force', true) rebuilds both unconditionally.
  report = buildDocumentation('Steps', "reference") does only the first.

  This is the single point of access for building documentation, beside
  buildMex for the gateway and runTestSuite for the suite. Together those
  three are every build step the toolbox has, and setupReferenceSystem drives
  all of them.

INPUT ARGUMENTS

Name   Size   Type   Units  Description
  ----   ----   ----   -----  -----------
  -      -      -      -      No positional arguments.

OPTIONAL NAME-VALUE ARGUMENTS

Name          Size   Type      Default              Description
  ----          ----   ----      -------              -----------
  ToolboxRoot   1x1    string    the installed root   Distribution folder.
  Output        1x1    string    the doc folder page  Reference page path.
  Steps         1xM    string    both                 "reference", "search".
  Force         1x1    logical   false                Rebuild even if current.

OUTPUT ARGUMENTS

Name                Size   Type     Units  Description
  ----                ----   ----     -----  -----------
  report              1x1    struct   -      What each step did.
  report.referenceFile 1x1   string   -      Path of the reference page.
  report.referenceAction 1x1 string   -      "written", "current", "skipped".
  report.searchFolder 1x1    string   -      Path of the search database.
  report.searchAction 1x1    string   -      "built", "current", "skipped",
                                             "failed".
  report.reason       1x1    string   -      Why, in one sentence.

METHOD

DECISION D33: THE FUNCTION REFERENCE IS GENERATED AND NEVER HAND-WRITTEN.
  Maintaining prose for eighty functions beside eighty help blocks saying the
  same thing is the two-sources-of-truth pattern this toolbox rejects
  everywhere else, and it is the copy that rots first, because the help text
  is what developers actually read.

  The generator is safe because the input format is guaranteed rather than
  hoped for: toolbox_provenance_helpTemplateIsFollowed asserts that every
  package file carries the H1 line and all eight mandatory section headings,
  so a file this parser cannot read fails a test before it reaches here.

  THE ARGUMENT BLOCKS ARE EMITTED AS PREFORMATTED TEXT, NOT RE-TABULATED.
  The help text already IS the table, laid out in fixed-width columns that a
  reader of the help sees in exactly that form. Re-parsing those columns into
  HTML cells would add a second layout free to disagree with the first, for
  no gain a reader would notice, and would fail on the one file whose
  description wraps in an unforeseen way. Preformatted output cannot
  misrepresent its input.

  The layer grouping is read from Contents.m, the same single authority the
  layering test reads (decision D41), so a function missing from the manifest
  is missing from the reference and fails a test rather than disappearing
  quietly.

  STALENESS IS DECIDED BY MODIFICATION TIME, not by a stored hash. A hash
  would be a fourth artefact to keep current and would itself go stale; the
  file system already answers the question. The reference is rebuilt when any
  package file is newer than the page, and the search database when any page
  or helptoc.xml is newer than the database.

  A FAILED SEARCH DATABASE WARNS AND DOES NOT ERROR. Search is a convenience
  on top of pages that are already readable, so its absence is a degraded
  state and not an impossible one, and builddocsearchdb depends on release
  details this toolbox does not control.

ACCURACY

Not applicable: the function transforms text and computes nothing.

ERRORS

referenceSystem:buildDocumentation:packageNotFound
  referenceSystem:buildDocumentation:docFolderNotFound
  referenceSystem:buildDocumentation:outputNotWritable

REFERENCES

Handover Part E and decision D33. MathWorks, "Display Custom
  Documentation", for info.xml, helptoc.xml and builddocsearchdb.

EXAMPLE

report = referenceSystem.buildDocumentation();
  disp(report.referenceAction)

KEYWORDS

documentation, function reference, help, generator, build, search database

See also BUILDMEX, RUNTESTSUITE, MEXINFO.

referenceSystem.runTestSuite

Run the referenceSystem test suite and write one plain-text report.

SYNTAX

results = runTestSuite() runs every test in the test folder of this toolbox and
  writes the report described in handover section D.3 to
  referenceSystem_testReport.txt in the current folder.

  results = runTestSuite(Tags=t) runs only the tests carrying one of the tags in t.
  results = runTestSuite(Output=f) writes the report to the file f.
  results = runTestSuite(Verbose=true) additionally prints framework output.
  [results, reportFile] = runTestSuite(...) also returns the full path written.

INPUT ARGUMENTS

Name             Size   Type      Units  Description
  ----             ----   ----      -----  -----------
  options.Tags     1xM    string    -      Test tags to select, or 'all'
                                           (default). Permitted: contract,
                                           reference, precision, speed,
                                           vectorization, layering,
                                           provenance, diagnostics,
                                           concurrency, convergence.
  options.Output   1x1    string    -      Report file name or path. Default
                                           'referenceSystem_testReport.txt'.
                                           A bare name is written to the
                                           TOOLBOX ROOT, not to pwd, so the
                                           report does not move with the
                                           current folder. The resolved path
                                           is returned as writtenTo.
  options.Verbose  1x1    logical   -      Print framework progress to the
                                           command window. Default false.
  options.Instruments
                   1x1    logical   -      Append the PLATFORM INSTRUMENTS
                                           section: the host probe and the
                                           hot-path profile, so ONE report
                                           per machine answers both "is the
                                           toolbox correct" and "how does
                                           this machine behave". Costs a few
                                           seconds. Default true; set false
                                           for a pure test run, and it is
                                           false in the nested run inside
                                           TestDispatch.
  options.ScratchDir
                   1x1    string    -      Second location for the probe's
                                           file measurement. Default tempdir.
                                           ON WINDOWS THE DEFAULT SITS UNDER
                                           THE USER PROFILE and cannot
                                           separate a scanner from the
                                           platform; name a path outside it.

OUTPUT ARGUMENTS

Name         Size   Type              Units  Description
  ----         ----   ----              -----  -----------
  results      1xN    matlab.unittest.  -      One TestResult per selected
                      TestResult               test; empty if none matched.
  reportFile   1x1    string            -      Absolute path of the report
                                               that was written.

METHOD

The suite is built with TestSuite.fromFolder over the test folder, which
  lies outside the package: the test classes call referenceSystem.* exactly as
  a user does, so a function accidentally left out of the namespace fails the
  suite instead of resolving through an import (section C.5 rule 4).

  Benchmark and convergence rows are not produced by this function. A test
  that measures something logs one pipe-separated record through
  testCase.log, the framework records it, and this function parses those
  A WARNINGS section lists every test that raised a MATLAB warning it did not
  suppress. Warnings appear in no TestResult, so before revision 1.58 they
  reached the console and nowhere else: a real gimbal-lock warning survived
  four S4 runs unreported and was found by a reader watching the console. The
  count in SUMMARY is of TESTS THAT WARNED, not of warnings - lastwarn keeps
  only the most recent - and the section says so rather than implying a total
  it does not have.

  records into the BENCHMARKS, CONVERGENCE and NOTES sections. The measuring test
  therefore owns its numbers and the runner owns only their layout. The record
  formats are built by TestPerformance.benchmarkRecord and
  TestPerformance.convergenceRecord, the single authority for them.

  The report is one UTF-8 file with LF line endings, fixed-width columns and
  no colour, so it can be uploaded to a later session and parsed again. Test
  durations are given to three decimals; benchmark medians to six, because a
  single-epoch call is measured in microseconds.

  A TEST THAT IS MEANT TO FILTER SAYS SO. Two checks in this suite cannot run
  on a healthy machine - one needs a MATLAB release that still permits a
  namespace folder on the path, the other needs a transfer slow enough to time
  out - and until revision 1.204 the report described them in the framework's
  words, "assumption was not met" and "assumeTrue failed". Read as intended
  that says nothing is wrong; read at a glance it says two things are, on
  every run, for the life of the toolbox. The runner therefore carries an
  allow-list of the tests whose assumption is EXPECTED to fail, with the
  standing reason for each. They are labelled SKIP, counted separately in the
  summary, and listed under INTENTIONALLY SKIPPED with the reason instead of
  the boilerplate. Every OTHER filtered test keeps the INC label and its own
  section: an absent optional toolkit is a fact about the machine that the
  reader needs, and a blanket rename would have buried it. Adding a test to
  the allow-list is a claim that its premise cannot exist anywhere, not that
  it happens to be filtered today.

  The header states the toolbox version from toolboxVersion, the MATLAB
  release and platform, and the MEX and leap-second state from mexInfo. Before
  the gateway is built, mexInfo reports that fact and the report is written
  anyway: a harness that only works once the toolbox is finished is not a
  harness.

  Every call to a sibling package function is qualified and is made in this
  main function, never in a local one: a namespace folder is not on the path,
  so an unqualified sibling name does not resolve anywhere in a package file,
  and the local formatting functions receive what they need as arguments
  rather than fetching it.

  The second output exists because no library function in this toolbox prints
  (design principle 10), so the location of the report would otherwise be
  undiscoverable whenever the default is used.

ACCURACY

Not applicable to the runner itself; it computes no physical quantity.
  Durations are wall-clock values reported by the test framework and inherit
  its resolution, typically a few microseconds, which is why the speed tests
  of section D.1 take a median over repetitions rather than a single timing.

ERRORS

referenceSystem:runTestSuite:testFolderMissing

WARNINGS

None raised by this function. It RECORDS the warnings raised by the tests
  it runs, in the WARNINGS section of the report; see WarningRecordingPlugin.
      The test folder is not where the package expects it, next to the
      package folder. Reinstall the toolbox as one distribution folder.
  referenceSystem:runTestSuite:cannotWriteReport
      The report file could not be opened for writing.

REFERENCES

Handover section D.1 (test categories), D.2 (structure), D.3 (this report).

EXAMPLE

results = referenceSystem.runTestSuite();
  contractOnly = referenceSystem.runTestSuite(Tags="contract", Verbose=true);

KEYWORDS

test, suite, report, unittest, benchmark, convergence, harness

See also MEXINFO, TOOLBOXVERSION.

referenceSystem.teardownReferenceSystem

Release the toolbox so its folder can be deleted.

SYNTAX

status = teardownReferenceSystem() unloads the MEX gateway, closes any file
  the toolbox left open, leaves the folder if it is the current one, and
  removes the toolbox root from the search path. It then MEASURES whether the
  folder is still held and reports what is holding it.

  status = teardownReferenceSystem('Save', true) also calls savepath, so the
  removal survives the MATLAB session.

INPUT ARGUMENTS

This function takes no positional arguments. It acts on the toolbox it is
  itself part of, located from mfilename('fullpath'), so there is no root to
  pass in and no way to point it at the wrong installation.

OPTIONAL NAME-VALUE ARGUMENTS

Name   Size   Type      Default   Description
  ----   ----   ----      -------   -----------
  Save   1x1    logical   false     Call savepath after removing the root.

OUTPUT ARGUMENTS

Name                 Size   Type      Units  Description
  ----                 ----   ----      -----  -----------
  status               1x1    struct    -      What was released and what was
                                               not.
  .toolboxRoot         1x1    string    -      The folder this function acted
                                               on.
  .mexWasLoaded        1x1    logical   -      Whether the gateway was in
                                               memory when called.
  .mexStillLoaded      1x1    logical   -      Whether it is STILL in memory
                                               after the unload attempt. This
                                               is the one field to read.
  .filesOpenInTree     1xN    string    -      Files inside the tree that are
                                               open. REPORTED, NOT CLOSED; see
                                               METHOD.
  .leftCurrentFolder   1x1    logical   -      Whether the current folder had
                                               to be changed.
  .removedFromPath     1xN    string    -      Path entries removed.
  .pathSaved           1x1    logical   -      True if savepath succeeded.
  .folderIsHeld        1x1    logical   -      Measured, see METHOD. NaN-free:
                                               false also when the test does
                                               not apply, and .heldBy says so.
  .heldBy              1x1    string    -      Plain-language account of what
                                               still holds the folder, or ""
                                               when nothing does.

METHOD

A folder cannot be deleted on Windows while anything inside it is open, and
  the usual holder is not a file but a LOADED LIBRARY: mexGateway.mexw64 is a
  DLL mapped into the MATLAB process, and Windows locks a mapped DLL's folder
  and every ancestor of it. That is why the whole toolbox tree resists
  deletion when only one file deep inside it is in use. On Linux and macOS the
  problem does not arise - a loaded .so may be unlinked - so this function is
  close to a no-op there and says so rather than pretending to have worked.

  THE STEPS, IN THE ORDER THEY MUST HAPPEN.

  1. REPORT the files open inside the tree, and close none of them. The list
     comes from openedFiles, not from fopen('all'), which was removed in
     R2025a and raises on R2026a; see listOpenFiles for the shim. The first
     version of this function closed them, on the reasoning that a file inside
     the tree is the toolbox's business. THAT WAS WRONG AND THE SUITE PROVED
     IT ON THE FIRST RUN: runTestSuite writes testReport.txt into the current
     folder, which during a run is the toolbox root, so this function closed
     the report file of the harness that was calling it, out from under a live
     fprintf. Both of its own contract tests errored.

     The rule that replaces it: A TEARDOWN CLOSES WHAT IT OPENED AND NOTHING
     ELSE. A file handle belongs to the code that created it, which alone
     knows whether it is finished with it; this function cannot know that and
     must not guess. The names are reported in .filesOpenInTree and named in
     .heldBy so the caller can act, which is the whole of what an outsider is
     entitled to do. fclose('all') would have been the same error, larger.

  2. Leave the folder. Windows locks a process's current directory, so if pwd
     is inside the tree nothing else will help. The function moves to the
     parent of the toolbox root, never to a folder chosen for the caller.

  3. Unload the MEX. `clear mex` is used and NOT `clear all`: they are not
     synonyms for this purpose, `clear all` additionally destroys the caller's
     workspace, and this function has no business doing that.

  4. Remove from the path, optionally saving.

  5. MEASURE, and this is the point of the function. Success is not inferred
     from the four steps having run without error - that is obligation O9's
     failure mode exactly, and this toolbox has already been bitten by it once
     in buildMex. Two independent measurements are taken:

       inmem   - the second output of inmem lists the MEX files in memory. If
                 the gateway is still there after `clear mex`, it did not
                 unload, and no amount of path tidying will free the folder.
       fopen   - the gateway file is opened with 'r+', read and write without
                 truncation. Windows maps a loaded DLL with read sharing only,
                 so this open FAILS while the library is loaded and succeeds
                 once it is not. The file is closed immediately and nothing is
                 written. On Unix the open succeeds either way, so the result
                 is reported as not applicable rather than as a pass.

  WHAT THIS FUNCTION CANNOT RELEASE, and will name in .heldBy instead:

    - A file from the tree open in the MATLAB Editor. The Editor holds its own
      handle and nothing in the language can close it. testReport.txt is the
      usual offender, because it is the file one has just been reading.
    - Any file another part of the program has open under the tree. These are
      listed in .filesOpenInTree rather than closed, for the reason in step 1.
    - The Current Folder browser pointed inside the tree, which is a separate
      handle from pwd.
    - MATLAB's folder change notification, which takes a directory handle on
      Windows for folders it has seen on the path. Setting the "Enable folder
      change notification" preference off, or restarting MATLAB, releases it.
    - A MEX that will not unload because its OpenMP runtime keeps a live
      thread pool. MEASURED ON R2026a WITH MinGW64 AND OpenMP LIVE ON 16
      THREADS: "clear mex" did NOT release the mapped gateway, and the linker
      could not overwrite it until MATLAB was restarted. So restarting is not
      the last resort here, it is the reliable cure, and "clear mex" is the
      cheap thing worth trying first rather than the fix.

  Restarting MATLAB releases all of them, which is worth saying plainly: this
  function exists so that a restart is not needed, not to claim it never is.

  WHY THIS LIVES INSIDE THE NAMESPACE AND setupReferenceSystem DOES NOT. Setup
  has to be callable before the toolbox root is on the path, so it sits at the
  root as a plain function. Teardown can only be wanted after setup has run,
  which is exactly when referenceSystem.* resolves, so it belongs with the
  rest of the package. The asymmetry is deliberate rather than an oversight,
  and it is the one place in the toolbox where a matched pair of operations
  does not live in a matched pair of places.

  STEP 3 HAS NOW BEEN MEASURED AND THE ANSWER IS NO. The first version of this
  help said that whether "clear mex" unloads a MEX-file in every release and
  state was a MATLAB semantic its author had not measured, and that step 5
  existed so the function would ask rather than claim. It asked, on R2026a
  with OpenMP live on 16 threads, and "clear mex" did not release the gateway:
  a rebuild in the same session still failed to link, and only restarting
  MATLAB freed it. The function is unchanged by that - it already reported
  what it found - but the help no longer implies the unload usually works.
  This is what building the measurement into the function bought, and it is
  the argument for doing so again elsewhere.

ACCURACY

Not a numerical function. The one quantitative claim is the fopen probe, and
  its meaning is exact on Windows and absent elsewhere: a loaded DLL is mapped
  with FILE_SHARE_READ, so an open for writing is refused by the operating
  system rather than by a heuristic.

  The probe cannot distinguish a lock held by MATLAB from one held by another
  process - a virus scanner or a backup agent reading the same file will fail
  it too. That is a limitation of the measurement, not a fault in it, and
  .heldBy says so rather than naming MATLAB with unearned confidence.

ERRORS

referenceSystem:teardownReferenceSystem:rootNotFound

REFERENCES

MathWorks, clear - Remove items from workspace, freeing up system memory,
  MATLAB documentation; note that "clear all" and "clear mex" are not
  synonyms for unloading a MEX-file.
  MathWorks, inmem - Names of functions, MEX-files, classes in memory.
  Microsoft, LoadLibrary and DLL locking: a mapped image is opened with
  FILE_SHARE_READ, so the file cannot be written or deleted while mapped.

EXAMPLE

status = referenceSystem.teardownReferenceSystem();
  if status.folderIsHeld
      disp(status.heldBy)
  end

KEYWORDS

teardown, uninstall, unload, MEX, DLL, file lock, Windows, path, cleanup

See also SETUPREFERENCESYSTEM, BUILDMEX, MEXINFO.
